# Review log: Runtime SDKs assume one session per process

## Standing context

Changelog (compaction pass [prune.4], 2026-10-04, after non-spec round 7): earlier passes lifted spec rounds 1 to 19, non-spec rounds 1 to 10, the firing, reconcile, owner, owner-delegate, prune.1 to prune.3, and redesign entries, and the second spec loop's round 1. This pass lifted the non-spec rounds 1 to 7 FACTs and DECISIONs of the current ledger, plus the second spec loop's round 2 to 6 entries that a CORRECTS or a closed Open needs.
Applied CORRECTS: **No setup retarget** states that SPEC-3 Edit 16 is the §6.3 edit ([spec.6.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.1.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1]); **Client inventory** drops the deleted `main_test.go` predicate-dial row note ([non-spec.1.fix-G2.1], [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1], [non-spec.2.review-edit-sites.1]); **Gate tier-7a coverage** is keyed to attempt 2 with no attempt-1 `SignalDeadline` ([non-spec.1.fix-G2.2], [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]); **Go SDK test row** covers CODE-4 items 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, and 9 ([non-spec.4.fix-G3.1], [non-spec.4.fix-design-G3.1]); **Startup SLO cost** gains the SDK-warm half ([spec.4.fix-G1.1], [spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1]); **Gate test clauses** records the [prune.4] rule; **Deadline check** states SPEC-6 Edit 2's one `message` ([non-spec.14.review-mechanism.1]); Trap **No harness failure category** records that CODE-6 item 1 names no `timeout` category ([non-spec.18.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.18.fix-design-G1.1]).
Closed Opens: `session_started` after `session_end` during `OnCreate`, Row 2 ordering key, Docs Basic traces, Docs exit-0 sentence, Gate concurrency review, `MCPRuntime.Output` subscription, `RUNTIME_ERROR` catalog, Claim-register rows, and Claim-register disagreement, each with the ledger entry that answered it in its Settled or Trap successor. Rule-8 application point stays Open. The ledger OPEN on whether CODE-6 item 1's harness wait is satisfiable at S16 ([non-spec.16.review-test-coverage.1]) is closed by [redesign.9.harness-read.1], with Trap **Harness wait constraint** as its successor.
Falsified: every ledger FACT claiming the `SocketRuntimeProcess` fan-out never blocks the shared reader (see Settled **Socket fan-out stall**). Length: about 270 lines, over the 200 target, because the non-spec rounds 1 to 7 had not been lifted; nothing open was dropped to meet it. The ledger was edited only to mark the falsified FACTs.

### Settled

Scope and owner decisions.
- **Owner decisions 10.3.** (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md section 10.3): one runtime lifetime contract; `session_start` and `session_end` ride CH-MSGSOCK rather than CH-RUNTIMEOPS; no runtime capability declaration for sequential sessions; no backward-compatibility shims. Do not reopen.
- **Owner answers 2026-10-03.** Part B (SPEC-8, CODE-7, the nonce handshake) stays here; `session_start` omits `taskId` and the workspace path because both derive from `sessionId` (a field can be added if either derivation ever changes); per-session platform and connector MCP connections, and the "MCP tools stop working for a later session" symptom, belong to proposal 0084; this proposal's spec steps land before 0087's and 0084's. Do not reopen.
- **Manifest ordering to 0087.** The first-session manifest-ordering defect (BUILD-GAPS F-4.7.26) belongs to draft proposal 0087's supervisor. Stage no fix here; state the dependency only. Exit-code wording (F-4.7.27) is also 0087's (summary decision 13).
- **Plan scope.** The claimed plan item is gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md §10.2 Phase 2 "Runtime-SDK proposal", with §10.3 "One runtime lifetime contract" and the Phase 0 F-4.7.25 and CH-RUNTIMEOPS nonce notes; every deliverable is staged or recorded as declined. Plan §10.6 "travels with the manifest-ordering fix" is stale (decision 25 records it); the "0090" at plan line 918 is a withdrawn R6 draft.
- **"Stream" meaning.** "Stream" in the §28.5.3 card means one session's logical stream multiplexed over the pod's one connection. The carrier is "the connection or loop" (sidecar connection, embedded runtime loop). Never write "stream" for the carrier (spec.1.fix-G1).
- **Open decisions.** The summary's `## Open decisions for human to make` reads "None."; both 2026-10-04 owner answers are staged (decisions 28 and 29) ([f4.cleanup]).
- **Cross-channel ordering (owner 2026-10-04).** `session_start` is acknowledged. A runtime that keeps per-session context, or that has opened `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`, writes `session_started` on CH-MSGSOCK once the session's context exists, with `error` when creation failed (SPEC-3 Edit 15 **Outbound: `session_started`**, **Session errors.**). The adapter writes no session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame for a session before it reads that frame (SPEC-4 Edit 2, the rule's one home). When the runtime's CH-RUNTIMEOPS handshake has completed, the adapter waits for the frame inside the open sequence; a missing or errored acknowledgement writes `session_end`, takes no record, and fails the start on the existing error arm (SPEC-3 **Session frame writes.** acknowledgement-failure row). The adapter does not wait before the first `message`, because CH-MSGSOCK write order already orders it. `session_end` is not acknowledged (summary decision 9). The runtime-side hold-and-drop rule and the SDK wait options are removed. The conformance precondition is "read `session_started` before a session-scoped CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame" (SPEC-6 Edit 4), and the Full **CH-RUNTIMEOPS opening** row checks the acknowledgement (SPEC-6 Edit 5). The adapter-side wait and gate are CODE-9, which lands after CODE-4, CODE-5, and CODE-6. Do not reopen.
- **`DemoteSDK` fails closed (owner 2026-10-04).** When the `DemoteSDK` handler's wait for the slot serialization outlasts its request's deadline, it fails without writing `session_end` and tears nothing down. The **Session frame writes.** `DemoteSDK` deadline row is the one home; it separates the gateway caller (pod fails, replacement claimed) from the §6.1 SIGTERM caller (pod `terminated`); SPEC-3 Edit 12 keeps only "`DemoteSDK` is the exception" as a pointer; CODE-1 item 3 says each caller takes its existing failure branch ([spec.19.fix-design-G1.1], [prune.1]). Do not reopen.
- **Client inventory.** The CODE-7 **Client inventory.** table is the one list of in-tree dialers and acceptors of `CH-MSGSOCK` and `CH-RUNTIMEOPS`. A change to a caller edits its row; do not reintroduce a prose caller list in the Targets, item 5, item 7, or the Tests paragraph. TEST-1's gate-test peer reuses the inventoried `startRuntimeOps` helper (clause on that row). The `cmd/lenny-adapter/main_test.go` predicate dial, its TEST-1 row, and its inventory row are deleted with the injected predicate ([non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]).

Acknowledgement mechanism.
- **Ack-wait predicate home.** Written out only in **Outbound: `session_started`** rule 3 and the **Session frame writes.** table; SPEC-1 Edit 4, SPEC-7 §29.2 step 31, the Design paragraph, the summary, and CODE-9 cite rule 3. The `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` ordering is stated only in SPEC-4 Edit 2 ([prune.1]); Inbound `session_start` rule 1 ends "This order holds on that connection or loop only.", SPEC-4 Edit 2 cites that sentence, and Outbound `session_started` rule 5 points at the `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` **Messages.** bullet ([spec.1.fix-G2.1], second spec loop).
- **"Hold" definition.** Defined once, as the lead of **Inbound: `session_start`** rule 3: a runtime holds a session from its read of `session_start`, including during context creation, until its read of `session_end`; `session_end` rule 2 releases a context still being created once creation ends; the `session_started` lead no longer says "once the runtime holds the session" ([spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Deferral bound.** SPEC-4 Edit 2 drops a deferred frame when "the session's start fails, the session ends, or a bound the adapter places on the deferral elapses first"; per-path bounds stay only in CODE-9 item 3, whose list ends with zero-bound callers using `SessionStartAckTimeout` ([spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]).
- **Startup SLO cost.** The open sequence's `session_started` wait counts toward the §6.3 agent_session_start phase and `lenny_session_startup_duration_seconds` (StartupLatencyBurnRate). On SDK-warm, the `session_start` write and the rule-3 wait run inside `ConfigureWorkspace` on the claim path (SPEC-3 Edit 16) ([spec.1.review-performance.1], [spec.4.fix-G1.1]).
- **No pod-wide lock in `StartSession`.** It holds only `s.mu` for short sections and the per-slot guard, so the acknowledgement wait costs co-tenants nothing at the adapter ([spec.1.review-mechanism.1]).
- **`files_updated` is mid-session only.** `FinalizeWorkspace` sends it only on the `midSession` branch, so pre-start workspace preparation never waits on a not-started gate ([spec.1.review-mechanism.1], [spec.1.review-reliability.1]).
- **Gateway Shutdown budgets.** `ReleaseSlot` uses the caller ctx with deadline 0 and compensating `ShutdownReclaim` uses `slotCleanupBudget`, so a Shutdown racing a pending ack wait at default flags does not plausibly reach the §5.2 failed-act path; not filed ([spec.1.review-performance.1]).
- **Edge-cases residual outcome.** The outcome of a frame written before `session_end` and read after it is stated only in the proposal's spec-changes Edge cases bullet, not in staged spec text or DOCS-1; the per-frame bounds it cites (60 s `credentials_acknowledged` fallback, `INTERRUPT_TIMEOUT`, the §28.6 `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` row) already exist in the tree ([spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1]).
- **Who acknowledges.** A runtime that keeps per-session context or opened `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` writes `session_started`; the adapter waits only when the runtime's `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` handshake completed before the open sequence began. A Basic runtime with no per-session context, and the §15.4.4 Standard sample, are not obliged; the §15.4.4 Full sample gains a `session_start` arm (SPEC-4 Edit 7).
- **Wait placement.** The wait sits between the `session_start` write and the second rule-8 confirmation; an acknowledgement failure writes `session_end` before the start's error arm, so a `running` record implies the runtime holds the session ([owner.2026-10-04]).
- **Wait bound.** Adapter flag `--session-start-ack-timeout`, IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE default below the 10 s `ConfigureWorkspace` timeout and the §5.2 ten-second window (CODE-9 item 2); its register row (basis no-spec-value) goes in tests/registers/deployment-boundary-defaults.yaml (CODE-9 item 7). The gateway's `StartSession` carries no deadline.
- **Not-awaiting start.** A start that did not wait marks the session not awaiting; its `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frames flow ungated (SPEC-3 Edit 15 rule 3, SPEC-4 Edit 2, CODE-9 item 1). Whether the first pod-warm session is gated depends on dial timing, because `StartSession` never waits on the `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` handshake; rule 3 accepts that.
- **`startId`.** Required adapter-minted per-start correlator on `session_start`, echoed by `session_started`; rule 5 drops a `session_started` whose `startId` is not the waiting open sequence's. The adapter mints from a per-`Server` atomic counter (CODE-1 item 1 step c); non-waiting writers use a constant ("1" in the CODE-2 executor, "compliance-1" in the CODE-3 helper) ([spec.16.fix-G1.1]).
- **`session_end` unacknowledged.** An acknowledgement would order nothing; the residual (a frame written before `session_end` and read after it) is in the spec Edge cases (summary decision 9).
- **Ack gate.** CODE-9 item 1 specifies a gate held on the per-binding `slotState` beside `barrier`: created not started; reset per start (pending before step c when waiting, not awaiting after step c otherwise); read on a matching error-free `session_started`; failed on a failed start or an errored match; released in `deregisterSlotLocked` and by `DemoteSDK` before its `session_end` decision. Senders reach the gate only through `awaitSessionStarted(ctx context.Context, st *slotState) error` (CODE-9 item 3), which reads the gate of the entry its caller passes and has no no-entry branch. "Record" now means only the §4.7.1 rule-8 record ([redesign.8.ack-gate.1], [non-spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Ack gate corrections.** Released stays terminal and a reset leaves it unchanged; item 2 moves the gate to failed before `writeSessionEnd`. `awaitSessionStarted` takes the caller's `*slotState` and never looks the session up by id, because the slot key equals the session id and a successor attempt's entry under that key carries a new gate. It returns nil for read or not awaiting without consulting ctx, and errors on failed or released; a removed entry's gate reads released, so the function has no no-entry branch. Only the wait observes ctx ([redesign.8.ack-gate.2], [non-spec.10.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.10.fix-design-G1.1], [prune.3]).
- **Gate entry sources.** How each caller resolves the entry it passes is an IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE in CODE-9 item 3. Its constraint: each request resolves its `slotState` once, passes that pointer to every `awaitSessionStarted` call including the deferred `CompleteCheckpoint`, never re-resolves by session id, and makes no waiting call under `s.ops` or a per-slot guard. CODE-9 names no resolution helper and stages no helper signature change; item 3 keeps one line per caller naming the lock it must not hold ([prune.3]).
- **Waiter bounds.** Every gate waiter carries a deadline: checkpoint bounds `stream.Context()` by `CheckpointStart.deadlineMs`, interrupt uses the request context bounded by `deadlineMs` (moved from `ictx`, which is built after `s.ops.Begin`), `deadline_approaching` uses `remainingMs`, `files_updated` uses `SessionStartAckTimeout`, and a zero bound falls back to `SessionStartAckTimeout` (CODE-9 item 3 closing paragraph is the one home; [prune.2]).
- **`s.ops` users.** Only Checkpoint (keyed per session) and Interrupt (pod-wide) take the pod-level op lock; Shutdown and deregistration never do, so a call queued in `s.ops.Begin` can outlive its entry while a successor entry appears under the same key ([non-spec.10.review-mechanism.1]).
- **Rotation gate error.** `rotateProviderFull` maps only `context.DeadlineExceeded` to the 60 s fallback; an implementor routes a gate error to the fallback explicitly, as CODE-9 item 3 names. Not filed ([non-spec.10.review-mechanism.1]).
- **`FinalizeWorkspace` order.** It calls `awaitSessionStarted` and `SignalFilesUpdated` after releasing the slot guard, because a guarded waiter blocks Shutdown, `DemoteSDK`, and Resume from releasing the gate ([non-spec.8.follow-up-fix.1]).
- **`Output` fan-out.** `InProcessRuntime.Output` and `SubprocessExecutor.Output` get fan-out semantics so a cancelled wait subscription consumes nothing (CODE-9 item 6), with a tier-1 case per transport.
- **Step order.** CODE-9 is S18 after CODE-4, CODE-5, and CODE-6; TEST-1 is S19, CODE-7 S20, RECORDS-1 S21. CODE-1 drops `session_started` at Attach so an early acknowledgement reaches no client; the compliance harness keeps an interim response-first order from CODE-3 until CODE-6 switches to the `session_started` read.
- **Conformance precondition.** SPEC-6 Edit 4: a category reads `session_started` before a session-scoped `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frame; a process-scoped frame such as `lifecycle_capabilities` has no precondition. The Full **CH-RUNTIMEOPS opening** row checks the acknowledgement with `sessionId` and `startId` (SPEC-6 Edit 5); §18.8 counts (basic 7/7, full 12/12) stay true.
- **`session_started` schema lists.** It stays out of `sessionScopedFrames`, `adapterPopulatedFrames`, and `runtimeEmittedFrames`, and its `sessionId` keeps `minLength: 1` (SCHEMA-1 items 1 and 4).
- **`files_updated` fixture.** SCHEMA-1 item 3 adds `runtime-ops.files_updated.json` and its `runtimeOpsEventExamples` entry (tier-0 bijection test).
- **CODE-2 item 4 deleted.** `readResponse` already skips non-`response` frames and `Send` waits for nothing, so the item instructed no code ([non-spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **0085 impact.** The **Session frame writes.** `DemoteSDK` deadline row cites the §4.7.1 `ConfigureWorkspace` replacement clause that 0085 SPEC-3 edit (a) deletes; whichever lands second re-bases ([f4.impact-0085]).

Session frames and rules.
- **`session_start` ordering home.** **Inbound: `session_start`** rule 1, in both deployment models, before the §4.7.1 rule-8 confirmation; card Preconditions, §4.7.10, and the §29 traces point at it. On SDK-warm the frame is written while handling `ConfigureWorkspace` (§29.2 step 33 cites rule 1).
- **Connection-end rule home.** **Inbound: `session_end`** rule 1 states that the end of the connection or loop ends every session with no `session_end`; §4.7.10 does not.
- **Rollback `session_end`.** The rule-8 rollback writes `session_end` only when the adapter then holds no entry for the slot identifier; `rollbackUnconfirmedStart` re-reads under `s.mu` and writes after releasing it ([spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Late `llm_request_completed`.** `session_end` rule 2 admits a late `llm_request_completed` for a request started before `session_end`, because the in-flight counter resets only on `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` close; the decrement stays unconditional and only the tokens are dropped.
- **LLM frame scope.** `llm_request_completed` is session-scoped with a required `sessionId`; `llm_request_started` is process-scoped (pod-wide in-flight counter). CODE-3 item 5 keys the token sink by the frame's `sessionId` and keeps `Server.SoleSessionID` (other callers exist), updating its comment.
- **`llm.baseUrl` deleted.** No `session_start` successor; proxy mode reads `materializedConfig.proxyUrl` from the credential file (§4.7.11 item 4); CODE-1 deletes `ManifestLLM.BaseURL`; CODE-8 item 3 asserts its absence.
- **Credential rotation row.** SPEC-6 Edit 3 has the runtime re-read the credential file at the `credentialsPath` that `credentials_rotated` names.
- **Frame-list homes.** The CH-MSGSOCK frame set lives on the card Messages bullet; the minimal-schema list lives in the **Message schemas** preamble (SPEC-3 Edit 4); the §15.4 artifact bullet list and both §15.4.1 enumerations are deleted to pointers (Edits 8 and 10).
- **`shutdown` home.** "`shutdown` is never written at a session boundary" lives only in **Inbound: `shutdown`** (SPEC-3 Edit 6).
- **Idle `deadline_approaching`.** The SPEC-4 Edit 3 row states that a runtime writes no `response` when no `message` for the session is in flight.
- **Addressing scope.** The **Addressing.** rule stays scoped to Attach-delivered frames; SPEC-3 Edit 14 excludes `session_start` and `session_end` and must land with Edit 1.
- **Basic trace.** SPEC-3 Edit 11 inserts `session_start` (step 2) and `session_end` (before the drain `shutdown`), giving steps 1 to 12.
- **Shutdown row citation.** SPEC-1 Edit 6 retargets "The teardown writes no `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frame" from §15.4.2 to §28.5.3.
- **Response completion sentence.** SPEC-3 Edit 7 deletes the "exits zero" success sentence with no replacement; the `failed` sentence stays and is recorded as a pre-existing defect ([f1.resolve-response-error-failed]).
- **§29.4 order.** SPEC-7 Edit 4 puts the child FINAL_USAGE_REPORT at step 13 and `session_end` at step 14, matching the Shutdown row (flush, then `session_end`).
- **Slot serialization.** SPEC-3 Edit 12: each wait lasts no longer than the deadline bounding the holder's own work (the §5.2 ten-second window for the hold-timeout termination); `DemoteSDK` holds it from its `session_end` decision until it answers, and its own cleanup runs under that hold; an act performed without it counts as failed (fail-closed).
- **`DemoteSDK` write order.** `session_end` is written under the slot serialization before `sw.DemoteSDK` ([f1.resolve-demotesdk-session-end-order]).
- **No setup retarget.** No §5.1 or §5.2 per-session setup retarget is staged: §5.1, §5.2, and the proto `RunSetupRequest` comment keep "the session's first message"; the former SPEC-3 Edit 15 is deleted and the `session_started` edit is now Edit 15; SPEC-3 Edit 16 is the §6.3 SDK-warm hot-path edit (**SPEC-3 Edit 16**); SCHEMA-1 item 6 drops the first-message proto retarget but still retargets proto comments that route context through the manifest; DOCS-1 item 4 keeps setup at the first message and warns that `OnCreate` and `session_start` work delays `session_started`; DOCS-1 item 1 cites "SPEC-3 Edits 5 and 15" ([spec.1.fix-G1.1], [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]).
- **Task id equality.** One home: the staged `CreateRequest.TaskID` comment (SPEC-5 Edit 4); SPEC-2 Edit 3 cites it; SPEC-5 Edit 4 cites §8.8 only for "a Task is the protocol surface of a session".
- **`OnCreate` cardinality.** Runs per opening; the SPEC-5 Edit 1 Handler comment points at the **Inbound: `session_start`** rules; CODE-4 item 7 and CODE-5 item 4 cite it.
- **`taskId` derivation in code.** pkg/adapter/manifest.go sets `TaskID: in.sessionID`; Resume runs only on a replacement pod.

Handshake (Part B).
- **Redial rule.** A runtime whose connection the adapter closes before its first protocol frame re-reads the manifest and redials (SPEC-8 Edit 1); this is what makes Part B independent of 0084 and 0087 (decision 25).
- **Handshake scope.** Scoped to socket connections; stdin/stdout and embedded runs need no nonce; SPEC-8 Edits 2, 7, 8, and 10 retarget Basic text; Edit 14 deletes "entire Basic-level wire surface"; Edits 3 and 4 point both Timing bullets at the handshake paragraph.
- **No-manifest refusal.** SPEC-8 Edit 1 states "A connection accepted while no manifest is published is refused." ([prune.1.closures]).
- **MCP dial count.** SDK platform and connector MCP connections are dialed once per process (decision 18); per-session MCP belongs to 0084, and the 0084 impact row says so.
- **SDK MCP challenge.** SDK MCP clients (Go `connectMCP`, Python mcp.py, TS mcp.ts) answer `_lennyChallenge` (CODE-7 item 5).
- **Accept loop.** CODE-7 item 3 makes `SocketRuntimeProcess.acceptLoop` loop on handshake failure in every `SocketPeerAuth` mode; `RuntimeOps.Run` already re-accepts.
- **Manifest path.** The runtimekit handshake helper takes the caller's resolved manifest path and re-reads it on each dial; only `runtimekit.Open` and binary-minimal use `LENNY_ADAPTER_MANIFEST`, else `/run/lenny/adapter-manifest.json`.
- **SpawnPath tests.** Each sets the `Server` `ManifestDir` (or writes a manifest) and `t.Setenv("LENNY_ADAPTER_MANIFEST", ...)`; spawn inherits `os.Environ()`, and none of these tests is parallel (CODE-7 item 7).
- **Part B tests.** They live in the CODE-7 Tests paragraph and land with CODE-7.
- **SDK socket dials.** All three SDKs and `runtimekit.Open` dial the socket `LENNY_ADAPTER_SOCKET` names and fall back to stdin/stdout. `LENNY_ADAPTER_MANIFEST` is set only by the compliance harness; no pod sets it.
- **Go SDK import.** The Go SDK sits in the root module (no own go.mod), so importing pkg/runtimekit adds no module dependency.

SDK, harness, docs, and tests.
- **SDK session routing.** The frame loop removes a session on `session_end`; a later `session_start` for the same id creates a new state whose `OnCreate` waits for the predecessor's `OnTerminate` (CODE-4 item 4).
- **SDK drain rule.** EOF and `shutdown` drain queued messages before `OnTerminate`; only `session_end` discards them (CODE-4 item 5).
- **`Run` waits for handed-off sessions.** CODE-4 item 5: `Run` returns after every session goroutine has returned, including one item 4 removed from the routing table on `session_end`; the wait primitive is the implementor's. The TEST-1 Go row pins it through its CODE-4 item 5 subtest. Today's Go `Run` already waits for `OnTerminate`; the SPEC-5 Edit 1 `Run` comment stays true ([non-spec.10.fix-design-G2.1]).
- **Attach no-relay test.** A tier-1 sentence in CODE-1 item 4, beside `TestAttachRelaysAProtocolLevelFrame_spec_28_5_3`, run with `HeartbeatInterval` unset; it lands with CODE-1 because TEST-1 (S19) is too late for the S14 to S18 window ([non-spec.10.fix-G3.1]).
- **Attach demux reach.** `deliverToSession` passes every frame type outside `sessionScopedFrameTypes` to every Attach stream on the pod, so an unconsumed `session_started` reaches every co-tenant's client when heartbeats are off ([non-spec.10.review-test-coverage.1]).
- **TEST-1 row 1 ack-failure variant.** "A start answered only by a `session_started` carrying an earlier start's `startId`" replaces "withheld"; it subsumes withholding and discriminates the `startId` match; pure withholding stays covered by row 3's item 2 arm and row 2 ([non-spec.10.fix-G3.1]).
- **Gate tier-7a coverage.** Extends TEST-1 row 2 (`TestSessionFramesStayPairedAcrossReclaimAndRetry_spec_28_5_3`) through the inventoried `rotationgate.NewPodAdapter` and `rotationgate.DialPeer`, asserting no early `deadline_approaching` and each `SignalDeadline` returning within its bound. The row issues no `SignalDeadline` against attempt 1; it signals after attempt 2's bind, concurrent with attempt 2's `StartSession`, and keys the ordering clause to the `session_started` answering attempt 2's `session_start`, because a frame written under attempt 1's acknowledgement is the Edge-cases residual. It blocks on `s.Lifecycle.WaitHandshake` with a positive wait before attempt 2's `StartSession` ([non-spec.1.fix-G2.2], [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]). `NewPodAdapter` sets no `Runtime`; the test sets its recording `RuntimeProcess` on `Server.Runtime`.
- **Post-`session_end` writes.** A per-`sessionState` ended mark, set under the stdout writer's lock when the loop reads `session_end`, drops later `response` and `tool_call` writes; a dropped `tool_call` returns an error at once (CODE-4 item 4).
- **SDK `Tools`.** `Tools` is the process-scoped dialed MCP connection set in every SDK; each SDK's credential-path option (Go `WithCredentialsPath`, Python `RunOptions.credentials_path`, TS `RunOptions.credentialsPath`) is deleted (decision 22).
- **CredentialBundle layout.** CODE-4 item 8 decodes the §4.7.11 item 4 `providers[]` layout with `MaterializedConfig` undecoded; CODE-5 item 3 and CODE-6 item 7 point at it; fixtures move in CODE-4 item 12, CODE-5 item 8, and CODE-6 item 7.
- **`credentialsPath` predicate.** `writeSessionStart` sets it only when the entry records `slotState.assigned`, which only `assignCredentialsSlot` sets after the file is written.
- **Compliance prefix home.** CODE-3 item 4 (S11) owns the harness `session_start` prefix; CODE-6 item 1 points at it. Current SDKs and echo-concurrent ignore an unknown `session_start`, so S11 is safe before S14 to S16.
- **`session_lifetime` check.** SPEC-6 Edit 1 is the one home of the A/B/C/D sequence; it replaces `sequential_messages_handled`, and probe.go `checkLevel` maps `session_lifetime` to Basic.
- **Deadline check.** CODE-6 item 3 reads every frame through the closing `heartbeat_ack` and fails on a second `response` after `deadline_approaching`. SPEC-6 Edit 2 sends one `message` after the `session_started` read, then `deadline_approaching`, and fails on any other `response` for the session after `deadline_approaching` ([non-spec.14.review-mechanism.1] CORRECTS).
- **Caller-level tests.** CODE-3 item 4 drives each sender caller for one of two bound sessions, so a caller resolving `SoleSessionID` fails.
- **Credential isolation tier.** Cross-session isolation is pinned at tier 10 in credential_path_resolution_conformance_test.go, one case per SDK.
- **Python and TS tests.** Neither SDK has a unit-test runner; their cases are tier-3 subtests in tests/tier3_contract/sdks/runtime_sdk_test.go.
- **Scaffold templates.** Each lands in the deliverable whose commit breaks it (Go cells CODE-4, Python and typescript-chat CODE-5, binary-minimal CODE-7); TS minimal and coding templates compile unchanged.
- **TEST-1 matrix row.** Its non-happy-path cell points at **Session frame writes.** (one subtest per row) plus only the non-table cases.
- **Gate test clauses.** TEST-1 row `TestSessionStartedGatesRuntimeOpsAndBoundsTheStart_spec_28_5_3` is one subtest per rule CODE-9 items 1, 2, and 3 state (transitions, `awaitSessionStarted` branches, caller bounds and error branches, start-failure arms, the subscription rule, the zero-value default, and each IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE constraint), each under a fixture in which a violating implementation fails, plus three named scenarios: `Shutdown` with an expired guard and hold-timeout pass 1 while a sender waits, and `Checkpoint` and clean `Interrupt` queued in `Begin` with a successor ([prune.2], [prune.4]). The fixtures that make each subtest discriminate are in Trap **Gate test fixtures**. The Go SDK row asserts the repeated `session_started` carries the duplicate's own `startId`.
- **Docs sweeps.** DOCS-1 item 9 is a docs/-wide predicate (manifest as carrier, "no frame signals a session's end", session opened at its first message); item 5 is a docs-wide predicate for Basic manifest statements and states the challenge answer and redial; item 1 adds a `session_start` field table following SPEC-3 Edit 5.
- **Nonce gate retarget.** The whole `intraPodNonceSites` retarget lives in DOCS-1 item 8 (S9); the gate is red between S2 and S9 by design.
- **Checklist tiers.** Tier 5 on S12 and S16; tier 4 on S16; S11 carries tiers 4 and 7a; S19 carries tiers 0, 1, 3, 4, 7a, and 10, with no tier 5 or 8, matching TEST-1's rows ([non-spec.10.review-test-coverage.1] CORRECTS).
- **No Kubernetes surface.** No staged edit touches a CRD, status, controller, finalizer, webhook, or RBAC; every mechanism is intra-pod or adapter-local.
- **No store writes.** No etcd, Postgres, or Redis write is added; the slot serialization is per slot identifier.
- **Direct-mode self-report.** Runtime-chosen `sessionId` on `llm_request_completed` is within the accepted §11.2 direct-mode residual risk (single-tenant or development, tenant-pinned pods); proxy-mode counts are gateway-authoritative ([spec.1.review-security.1] USEFUL).
- **Go SDK test row.** TEST-1's Go SDK row is one subtest per behavior of CODE-4 items 1 (frame loop), 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, and 9, plus three verbatim discriminating clauses (differing-`startId` duplicate, retry that dispatches and answers, `context.Background()` late `ToolCall`). Item 1 was restored after [prune.3] left the cross-session non-blocking loop unpinned in all three SDKs ([prune.3], [non-spec.4.fix-G3.1]).
- **Spec anchors resolve.** At the second spec loop's round 1 every staged anchor and link target resolved (whitespace-normalized) except the known multi-site and `//`-spanning ones in Trap **Anchor matching**; the spec tree last changed 2026-10-03 (9d854ecd6) ([spec.1.review-applicability.1], [spec.1.review-citations.1], [spec.1.review-fresh.1]).
- **§29 renumbering has no inbound citers.** No spec/, docs/, or tests/ text cites a §29.2, §29.4, or §29.6 step number from outside spec/29; within §29.2 only "steps 24 through 29" is edited ([spec.1.review-applicability.1]).
- **Staged-site sweeps.** Every `terminate`, per-session manifest field, CH-MSGSOCK frame enumeration, manifest-delivery sentence, and nonce-handshake wording site in spec/ is staged; re-grep before refiling any ([spec.1.review-edit-sites.1], [spec.1.review-client-surface.1]).
- **Client surface.** No gateway-external client surface (REST, OpenAPI, MCP lenny/* tools, A2A, OpenAI-completions, CRDs) is touched; the client-facing surfaces are runtime-author ones ([spec.1.review-client-surface.1]).
- **Tier-11 nonce gate phrases.** The gate pins three phrases (§4.7.5 "authoritative for the session whose start last wrote it", §6.1 "rewrites the pod-global adapter manifest before each session's runtime start" and "before that session's binary is spawned"); SPEC-2 Edits 1 and 8 delete them and DOCS-1 item 8 retargets both sites ([spec.1.review-applicability.1]).
- **Frame-identifier gate delimiter.** The gate ends a card at the next line starting with `**` and reads only json fences; each staged frame card puts its fence before the next bold lead-in ([spec.1.review-applicability.1]).
- **Nonce challenge scope.** §4.7.11 item 1 and the §13 boundary table state the nonce-only challenge for every new connection on the abstract sockets, so SPEC-8 Edits 9 and 11 drop no MCP challenge ([spec.1.review-security.1]).
- **Kept-process residual state.** Per-session state left in a kept process (failed `session_end` write, `DemoteSDK` deadline failure) is inside the §5.2 process-level isolation acknowledgment ([spec.1.review-security.1]).
- **`llm.apiKeyEnv` narrowing.** SPEC-2's row narrows "sets this env var in its own process" to per-session client or subprocess env, which is stricter ([spec.1.review-security.1]).
- **Control-plane consequence.** The only control-plane consequence the staging cites is the pre-existing §4.7.1 `ConfigureWorkspace` row, quoted by the `DemoteSDK` deadline row; no new status write ([spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]).
- **Plan deliverable map.** Every deliverable of the claimed §10.2 Phase 2 step maps to a staged edit or a recorded divergence (server-first challenge is a non-goal; MCP once per process is decision 18) ([spec.1.review-plan-conformance.1]).

Second spec loop rounds 2 to 6.
- **Late `session_started` permitted.** **Inbound: `session_end`** rule 2 exempts "the `session_started` that **Outbound: `session_started`** obliges for a `session_start` read before the `session_end`"; rule 5 drops it by `startId`. CODE-4 item 2 conforms as staged, and item 4's ended mark stays limited to `response` and `tool_call`. Do not restate rule 1's condition in rule 2 or add an exception to rule 1 ([spec.2.fix-G1.1], [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **SPEC-3 Edit 16.** Two in-place replacements in §6.3: the SDK-warm hot-path parenthetical and the demotion-rate "SDK process start time" wording. Both anchors occur once in spec/06; no docs/, tests/, or scripts/ file mirrors them; the per-phase table and SLO note stay true ([spec.4.fix-G1.1], [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1], [spec.6.review-applicability.1]).

Gate mechanism facts (non-spec rounds 1 to 7).
- **Socket fan-out stall.** `SocketRuntimeProcess.broadcast` sends to each subscriber in turn, and `subscriber.send` blocks the shared reader while that subscriber's 64-slot feed is full and it is not done. One live, unread `Output` subscription therefore stalls every co-tenant's Attach stream, `heartbeat_ack` included (10 s ack window), until it is read or cancelled. The doc comments on `send`, `broadcast`, and the pump that say a full buffer blocks only that subscriber are wrong. Every ledger FACT claiming otherwise is falsified ([non-spec.1.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.1.review-single-source.1], [non-spec.7.review-client-surface.1], and the "not siblings" clause of [non-spec.7.review-fresh.1]). EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:166-203, :405-416 ([non-spec.1.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.7.review-mechanism.1]).
- **Subscription rule.** CODE-9 item 2 decides once, first, whether the start waits; only a waiting start subscribes, before step c; the sequence cancels `waitCtx` on every return, including a step-c write failure. The [non-spec.1.review-performance.1] rejection ("a deferred cancel makes the window microseconds") was wrong as staged ([non-spec.4.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **`Output` scope.** `SocketRuntimeProcess.Output` ignores its session argument, so the wait reads and discards co-tenant frames until its `startId` match. `InProcessRuntime` binds one session at a time, so the gate's co-tenant cost exists only on the socket transport. `MCPRuntime.Output` returns the shared Attach channel; no `type: mcp` start waits, so it never subscribes ([non-spec.4.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.4.review-performance.1], [non-spec.1.review-citations.1]).
- **Single gate writer.** The open sequence's wait is the only writer that moves the gate out of pending; the CODE-1 item 4 Attach filter consumes `session_started` and writes no gate state. Read runs only from pending, so read-to-failed is unreachable and Failed's "unless already released" guard suffices ([non-spec.1.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Handshake predicate.** The start waits when `s.Lifecycle != nil && s.Lifecycle.WaitHandshake(ctx, 0)` as the sequence begins. A non-positive wait checks the current connection without blocking, and `resetConn` re-arms it per connection. `rotationgate.NewPodAdapter` and main.go both set `s.Lifecycle`, so no predicate field or main.go helper exists ([non-spec.1.fix-G2.1], [non-spec.2.review-fresh.1]). On `--runtime-bin` a previous child's handshake can still read true; in-tree SDK children answer anyway, not filed ([non-spec.7.review-reliability.1]).
- **Post-`Begin` re-check.** Checkpoint and a clean Interrupt wait before `s.ops.Begin` and re-check the same entry after `Begin` returns with a call that does not wait, where not started or pending is an error, because `Begin` can queue behind a co-tenant's upload while the entry is released and a successor starts ([non-spec.1.fix-G3.1], [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]).
- **Zero `SessionStartAckTimeout`.** A zero field selects a compiled default that `--session-start-ack-timeout` defaults to and overrides, as `CredentialsAckTimeout` and `HeartbeatAckTimeout` do; every `adapter.New` caller other than main.go leaves it zero ([non-spec.2.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]). Item 3's citations of the field mean its effective value. The register scan records a `flag.Duration` default as its source expression (a named constant is fine; an opaque `flag.Var` fails), and a defaults-register row alone registers the flag ([non-spec.3.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.4.review-edit-sites.1], [non-spec.7.review-edit-sites.1]).
- **Registry hooks.** `createSlotStateLocked` is the only `slotState` constructor and `deregisterSlotLocked` the only `delete(s.slots, …)`, reached only through `reclaimSlotLocked`, so every removal opens the reclaim hold and the Created and Released hooks cover every entry; no whole-registry reset exists. Four test files build `slotState` literals, so the gate's zero value must read as not started ([non-spec.1.review-citations.1], [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.4.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.7.review-fresh.1]).
- **Pending implies guard.** A pending gate exists only inside an open sequence, which holds the per-slot guard (CODE-1 item 1 step a, or Resume from before its claim). The guard is free only while the gate is not started: the claim sets `started` under `s.mu` and `StartSession` runs `Runtime.Start` before step a. A mid-session `FinalizeWorkspace` (`allowStarted` permits, does not require, a started entry) can reach a not-started gate and blocks on the guard during a pending one ([non-spec.4.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.4.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.5.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]).
- **Hold-timeout pass 1 and connection end.** Pass 1 (`deregisterStartedSessions`) runs without the slot guard after `CloseListener` closes the connection; pass 2 takes the guard on the 10 s window. On EOF `fanOut` marks the process ended and closes every subscriber, and `Output` then returns `errRuntimeConnectionEnded`, so a wait ends without the frame. The socket accepts one connection for the pod's life, and `SocketRuntimeProcess.Close` is a no-op, so CODE-1 item 2's `Runtime.Close` ends no co-tenant ([non-spec.1.review-performance.1], [non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1], [non-spec.7.review-citations.1], [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]).
- **Sender call sites.** Exactly six sender call sites exist, all in the five CODE-9 item 3 callers; no adapter-internal timer sends a session-scoped frame. Today only Interrupt (`s.ops` before its clean branch) and `FinalizeWorkspace` (guard held across `SignalFilesUpdated`) hold a lock at the call; Checkpoint resolves its `slotState` before `Begin` and reuses it for `barrier.link`. `SignalDeadline` takes `_ context.Context` and `ictx` is built after `Begin`, so item 3's bounds are new plumbing ([non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1], [non-spec.4.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.7.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.7.review-citations.1]).
- **Lock-path bounds.** `opLock.Begin` queues an interrupt behind a running checkpoint when no checkpoint is pending and returns `errOpBusy` otherwise. Rotation waits on the gate after `awaitInflightGate`, which polls and holds no lock, inside the 60 s `ackCtx`. The gateway bounds `SignalDeadline` by its interrupt deadline ([non-spec.2.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.4.review-performance.1], [non-spec.4.review-security.1], [non-spec.7.review-single-source.1]).
- **Start-failure path.** An acknowledgement-timeout start failure takes the shipped path: the base-mode bind reclaims the pod and the slot path records a slot failure. The gateway reclaims after a `ConfigureWorkspace` failure and calls no `DemoteSDK`; CODE-1 item 2 describes the §4.7.1 row rather than the tree ([non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]).
- **Gate concurrency examined.** Non-spec rounds 1, 4, 5, and 7 traced the gate under the mechanism, reliability, performance, and fresh lenses (wakeups, the Attach filter versus the wait, connection drop, bounded waits, co-tenant cost, lock placement). The findings were the unconditional subscription, the zero timeout, the lock-placement fixture, the failed-gate branches, and the multi-waiter test; all are staged.

Other non-spec facts (rounds 1 to 7).
- **Envelope test retarget.** `TestSubprocessEnvelopeCarriesTheAddressedSession_spec_28_5_3` reads the first captured `message` frame rather than line 0, because CODE-2's `session_start` becomes line 0 with the same `sessionId` (TEST-1 row for that file) ([non-spec.7.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.7.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Basic compliance prefix.** The Basic checks send a message with no `session_start`; after S14 an SDK answers `RUNTIME_ERROR`, a pre-existing SDK code absent from the proto catalog. CODE-3 item 4's prefix reaches every message-sending check, so `response_error_code_in_proto_catalog` holds ([non-spec.1.review-client-surface.1], [non-spec.4.review-client-surface.1]).
- **Claim-register rows.** Not filed: rows for new §28 statements are a plan §2.1 end state, and the remedy lands in tests/, outside the staging ([spec.4.review-plan-conformance.1], [non-spec.7.review-edit-sites.1]).
- **Docs gates at S9 and S10.** `compliance_suite_artifact_enumeration_test.go` stays green when DOCS-1 (S9) names the session frames and drops `terminate` before SCHEMA-1 (S10). No tier-11 pin outside DOCS-1's Targets is falsified ([non-spec.1.review-client-surface.1], [non-spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1], [non-spec.7.review-docs-alignment.1]).
- **Operator docs.** No operator or runbook page enumerates `StartSession` failure causes and no docs page lists lenny-adapter flags, so the acknowledgement-timeout failure and `--session-start-ack-timeout` have no docs companion; security.md and nonce-only-mode.md stay true after Part B ([non-spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1], [non-spec.7.review-docs-alignment.1]).
- **Socket dial inventory.** Every unix-socket dial outside the CODE-7 inventory targets an MCP socket, and every cited existing test name resolves (exclude scratchpad/ copies when re-checking) ([non-spec.4.review-citations.1], [non-spec.4.review-edit-sites.1]).
- **Security facts.** `RuntimeOps.Terminate` has no non-test caller, so CODE-3 item 3 removes no control; the credential file is rewritten before the gated rotation frame, so a gate error leaves no revoked credential live; `session_start` `llm` members carry no secret ([non-spec.1.review-security.1], [non-spec.7.review-security.1]).
- **SDK signature fit.** TS templates and examples declare `onTerminate(): void {}` and compile against the two-parameter interface; Python handlers do not and sit inside CODE-5's commit ([non-spec.4.review-client-surface.1], [non-spec.7.review-edit-sites.1]).
- **Schema discriminator.** The runtime-ops `$defs` set `additionalProperties: false` and the JSONL root discriminator has no mapping, so SCHEMA-1's `oneOf` additions need no discriminator edit ([non-spec.7.review-client-surface.1]).

### Traps

- **Spec-changes glob.** A `*spec-changes.md` glob also matches `*non-spec-changes.md`. Name the file in full when reading, grepping, or editing either one. An earlier sed misread also invented a second §4.7.5 manifest paragraph; there is one.
- **Heartbeat escalation.** Heartbeat escalation ends only that session's stream (pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go, attach.go hbHung case) and never closes the pod connection (§4.7.10 "No session's teardown, interrupt, or heartbeat escalation ... closes that connection"). §15.4.3 says a missed ack "ends the session"; keep the frame-writes table consistent with both.
- **Withdrawn: per-session SDK MCP dial.** Round 1 moved the SDK MCP dial after `session_start` per session; [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1] reverted it because the §4.7.6 `mcpNonce` row and §29.2 step 26a (gate-pinned) say a running server never re-arms. The tree does re-arm on each sole-occupant claim, which is 0084's to reconcile. Do not reintroduce a per-session dial.
- **Withdrawn: cross-channel hold.** Rounds 7 to 15 tried a runtime-side hold-and-drop with a runtime-chosen wait, then a write-order harness precondition, then a response-read precondition; each moved the race (MISTAKE of round 11: write order across stdin and the socket orders nothing, so a zero-wait runtime fails checkpoint and interrupt rows). The `session_started` acknowledgement replaced all three. Do not revive a hold, a wait floor, or a write-order precondition.
- **Withdrawn: lazy ack record.** A record created by a sender with no entry and adopted by the next start leaked (no removal rule, post-teardown senders, non-waiting adoption). The slotState gate replaced it; a pod-level map keyed by session id was rejected because a successor attempt could inherit waiters.
- **Withdrawn: "fail the pod on missed ack" and ack counting.** Both were rejected for `startId`: failing the pod reopens the owner decision and kills co-tenants; counting owed acks misattributes because rule 1 does not oblige an ack after `session_end`; the rule-8 bind token is shared when empty.
- **Rule-5 regression.** The old rule-5 clause "and that has no §4.7.1 rule-8 record" must not return beside the `startId` match; it let an errored ack for a recorded session be ignored.
- **`startId` echo on a repeat.** The echo is of the `session_start` being answered, including a repeated one; an SDK resending its cached first answer stalls the later start until timeout. The differing-`startId` duplicate clause in the TEST-1 Go row discriminates; do not collapse it to "written again".
- **Provenance sentence.** Do not reintroduce any sentence equating `session_start` members with deleted §4.7.6 rows: `llm.headers` never had a row (only §4.9 and CH-LLMPROXY), and `llm.baseUrl` is deleted without being a member. MISTAKE (round 16): adding `startId` falsified three summary statements; grep the summary for the old frame description whenever a wire member is added.
- **Predicate copies.** Do not restore a written-out ack-wait predicate or `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` ordering rule outside rule 3, the **Session frame writes.** table, and SPEC-4 Edit 2; five copies with different conjuncts were the cause of rounds 16 to 19.
- **`DemoteSDK` consequence copy.** Do not restore the consequence clause in SPEC-3 Edit 12; it was a second copy of the deadline row and had drifted (it said "fails the pod", false for the SIGTERM caller).
- **`SubprocessExecutor.Output` filter.** MISTAKE (non-spec round 8): CODE-2 item 4 staged a `session_started` drop in the executor's stdout reader. `lenny-adapter` accepts `--runtime-bin` with the runtime-ops socket, and CODE-9's wait reads the acknowledgement through that `Output`. Never filter there or in item 6's fan-out; the CODE-1 Attach filter keeps it from clients.
- **Sender before start.** A sender for a bound but not started session waits on the gate; do not let it pass, and do not add a `s.Runtime == nil` bypass to keep tests green (CODE-9 item 5 has the tests start the session). Do not enumerate the affected tests by name in item 5; only `rotationgate.NewPodAdapter` needs a structural change.
- **Guard holders and the gate.** Any gate caller that holds the per-slot guard deadlocks against Shutdown, `DemoteSDK`, and Resume; only `FinalizeWorkspace` held it among sender callers. Check the guard before adding a caller. Checkpoint and interrupt wait on the gate before `s.ops.Begin`, never under the pod-level op lock, and re-check it without waiting after `Begin` returns, because a wait under it blocks every co-tenant's checkpoint and interrupt (CODE-9 item 3, [prune.2]).
- **Attach filter guard.** CODE-1 item 4's `session_started` consume must not sit under the `hb != nil` guard of the `heartbeat_ack` branch, or the frame is relayed when heartbeats are off. The CODE-1 test must run with `HeartbeatInterval` unset, or the `heartbeat_ack` branch masks a filter placed under the guard and the test does not discriminate.
- **Gate test fixtures.** TEST-1 row 3's subtests discriminate only under these fixtures; the row states the rule and these reasons live here ([prune.4]).
  - Lock placement: run each caller against a not-started gate (session claimed, `StartSession` parked in `Runtime.Start`) on a concurrent pool, and assert `s.ops` and the slot guard stay acquirable. A pending gate implies the opener holds the guard, and a wait under a lock ends at the same bound with the same outcome, so only a co-tenant observer discriminates ([non-spec.4.fix-G2.1], [non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1]).
  - Released branch and queued-in-`Begin`: register a successor entry under the same id while the caller holds the old one (before `Begin` returns for the queued case), or a by-id implementation also errors and passes. The queued case needs one running co-tenant checkpoint and an empty pending set ([non-spec.10.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1], [non-spec.2.review-applicability.1]).
  - Rotation error branch: use a non-deadline gate error (failed); `rotateProviderFull` maps only `context.DeadlineExceeded` to the 60 s fallback, so assert the fallback with no `LEASE_REJECTED` ([non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1], [non-spec.4.review-test-coverage.1]).
  - Zero default: use an `adapter.New` Server with the field unset (as `rotationgate.NewPodAdapter` builds it); a delayed answer starts and a withheld one fails within item 2's bound, because immediate expiry passes row 2 vacuously ([non-spec.4.fix-G2.1]).
  - **Owner** constraint: two item 3 callers that take no `s.ops` before the wait wait at once, once per transition out of pending, each `ctx` outlasting the transition (for Failed, outlasting `SessionStartAckTimeout`); assert both return before either `ctx` ends, or a wake-one primitive passes ([non-spec.7.fix-G2.1], [non-spec.7.fix-design-G2.1]).
  - Subscription rule: the recording runtime logs each `Output` context; the returns at steps a and b precede the subscription, so the assertion is vacuous there. Do not move the subscription before step b to make it bite ([non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.7.review-single-source.1]).
- **Do not reinstate "moves only to released".** A not-awaiting or read gate can still move to failed when the open sequence's rule-8 confirmation fails after step c (CODE-9 item 1 Failed). CODE-9 item 3 makes no claim about a gate's later transitions; its IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE constraint (no waiting call under `s.ops` or a per-slot guard) carries the obligation that the derivation "never again becomes not started or pending" used to argue ([non-spec.10.fix-G1.1], [prune.3]). The ledger disagrees on whether a gate can return to pending ([non-spec.1.fix-G3.1] says Resume resets the same entry; [non-spec.7.review-mechanism.1] says the claim admits one open sequence per entry); the post-`Begin` call's non-waiting property is a stated constraint, so neither reading changes it.
- **Withdrawn: id-lookup gate with identity check.** Keeping `awaitSessionStarted(ctx, sessionId)` plus an "entry other than the caller's" check, a pointer parameter only for in-lock calls, and dropping the in-lock calls were all rejected for the entry pointer: two mechanisms for one predicate, a dual signature, or a removed attempt's frame reaching a successor before its `session_started`. Do not restore a nil-entry error branch either; `boundSlotState` already refuses an absent entry.
- **Vacuous `SignalDeadline` in 7a.** `SignalDeadline` returns Delivered=false with no frame when `s.Lifecycle == nil` or the peer did not declare `deadline_signal`, so a 7a sender that never reaches the gate passes vacuously. `rotationgate.DialPeer` already declares `deadline_signal`.
- **Tier-7a package limits.** Tier 7a runs only `./tests/tier7a_load_local/...`; no `pkg/` file carries the `load_local` tag, so a 7a test cannot reuse `pkg/adapter` helpers such as `startRuntimeOps` or call `awaitSessionStarted`. A gate race case drives the public sender RPCs (`SignalDeadline`, `Checkpoint`) and Attach through the exported `rotationgate` helpers.
- **Cross-channel residual in 7a ordering.** A frame written under attempt N's acknowledgement can legitimately arrive after attempt N+1's `session_start` (spec Edge cases residual of SPEC-4 Edit 2), so an assertion like "no ops frame between attempt N's `session_start` and `session_started`" flakes. Row 2 therefore signals only against attempt 2 (Settled **Gate tier-7a coverage**).
- **Python and TS inherit `Run` wait.** CODE-5 item 3 ("EOF and `shutdown` follow CODE-4 item 5") and the TEST-1 "The Go cases above, per SDK" row carry the handed-off-session wait; do not add a Python or TS restatement.
- **Released-gate window.** The released-versus-reset window is reachable only on the not-awaiting path (reset right after step c's write); hook the recording runtime's `session_start` write. "Failed after released" is not observable in behavior; do not assert internal state for it.
- **Schema list test.** Do not add `session_started` to any list in session_scoped_frame_address_test.go: `adapterPopulated` is wrong, `runtimeEmitted` fails the required-address test, and `sessionScoped` alone fails the `minLength` branch. Any `sessionScopedFrames` member outside `adapterPopulatedFrames` must leave `sessionId` without `minLength`.
- **Inventory before CODE-7.** TEST-1 (S19) lands before CODE-7 (S20); a TEST-1 case that dials either socket needs an inventory row or an inventoried helper. Package main cannot reuse pkg/adapter test helpers.
- **Caller lists drift.** When rewriting a generic caller phrase into an enumerated list, re-derive every dial site from the tree; round 6 dropped streaming-echo's own `dialLifecycleSocket` this way. The summary's CODE-7 file list drifts with the Targets.
- **Spec-lane steps.** A spec-lane step commits only spec/ files and runs no tests; never stage a tests/ or docs/ edit inside a SPEC-n deliverable. A spec step's tier list is not executed.
- **Tier-11 nonce gate.** intra_pod_mcp_nonce_doc_reconciliation_test.go anchors §29.2 step 26a by "The runtime reads the manifest and connects to the platform MCP" (move the step, never reword it), pins "started at most once per pod", "carried at the start that bound", and "does not re-arm" (0084 owns them), anchors "**MCP server security:**" and the §15.4.3 **Authentication.** sentence, and forbids "manifest's current" and "current manifest" at every site.
- **Frame-identifier gate.** The schema-driven tier-11 gate needs, per JSONL frame, a §28.5.3 **Inbound/Outbound** label and an adapter-contract.md heading containing "`<frame>` ---", each with a JSON fence carrying `sessionId`. Six agents noted the heading convention; adjacent headings set it, so do not refile.
- **Credentials doc pin.** adapter_manifest_credentials_path_doc_reconciliation_test.go pins lifecycle.md's manifest example `credentialsPath` line; DOCS-1 item 8's retarget lands with item 2's example deletion. Item 9's sweep does not reach fenced JSON.
- **Anchor matching.** SPEC-5 Edits 5 and 8 quote Go comments that span `//` prefixes; the CH-MSGSOCK and CH-RUNTIMEOPS nonce-only Timing sentence wraps differently per card; "Deployer acknowledgment (runtime process kept across sessions)")." occurs twice in spec/04 (SPEC-1 Edit 1 means the §4.7.10 one).
- **Edit numbering.** Edit numbers restart in every SPEC-n block; append a new edit rather than renumber. A scripted insert keyed on "**Edit 4 (" lands in SPEC-1 (a round made and reverted exactly that); anchor on "### SPEC-8 " or the full heading.
- **Task id citation.** Do not repoint the equality citation at another spec section; §7.2 and §8.8 were tried and neither states it. SPEC-5 Edit 4's §8.8 link is correct for its premise. The §8.8 example mismatch is pre-existing (Open).
- **`OnCreate` cardinality.** Never write "once per session" or "exactly once per sessionId" for `OnCreate` or `OnTerminate`; a same-pod retry reopens the same id on the same process. Assert `session_start`/`session_end` pairing.
- **Per-response completion.** MISTAKE (spec round 3): an "error absent means the response completes the task" sentence made every response terminal. Do not restore any per-response, per-turn, or per-session success sentence in **Error reporting via `response`.**
- **Ended mark key.** The post-`session_end` drop is keyed per `sessionState`, never per `sessionId` or routing-table absence; the TEST-1 retry case asserting "dispatches and answers" discriminates.
- **Late `ToolCall` test.** It discriminates only with a ctx not derived from the session ctx and a return bound far below the 30 s `AdapterTools` timeout; sync on A's `OnTerminate` returning first.
- **`Lifecycle.Send` filter.** Do not add a `sessionId` drop to the Go SDK `Lifecycle.Send`, the only path for `llm_request_*`; the in-flight counter leak returns. Do not retain bindings to keep late tokens.
- **Rollback under lock.** Do not write the rollback `session_end` under `s.mu`; `WriteEnvelope` blocks (unbuffered pipe, socket with no write deadline). The residual successor-admitted-in-the-gap interleave is accepted; do not answer it with a lock across I/O.
- **SDK-warm ordering.** Any sentence placing SDK-warm `session_start` after "`ConfigureWorkspace` succeeds" contradicts rule 1; any ordering edit greps every site that sequences `session_start`, including §29.2 and §29.6.
- **Redial independence.** Do not add a "depends on 0084/0087" precondition to SPEC-8 or reintroduce 0084 nonce pinning in the Impacts row (decision 25).
- **`llm.baseUrl` leak.** The JSONL schema allows additional properties, so only the CODE-8 absence assertion catches a leftover `baseUrl` if `ManifestLLM.BaseURL` survives.
- **Runtimekit path.** A helper that ignores the caller's path hangs every `WithManifestPath` test (polls with no deadline). Do not export the resolved path into the env or re-implement option-env-default precedence inside runtimekit.
- **Empty provider value.** Do not relax CODE-7 item 1's refusal of an empty nonce to make SpawnPath tests pass; publish a manifest in the test.
- **binary-minimal.** It must implement the handshake inline with the standard library (no requires in its go.mod, no SDK import); its socket branch and challenge answer are reached only by tier-5 `TestRuntimePublishJourney`.
- **Handshake discrimination.** A successful dial or accept does not prove the handshake; only a `response` to a `message` does. An unanswered MCP challenge decodes as an empty successful `initialize`; assert `tools/list`.
- **Handshake implementation.** Clear the 500 ms read deadline after the handshake; place the CH-RUNTIMEOPS nonce check before `serveConn` binds `lc.ready` and `lc.conn`; the fan-out scanner must read through the handshake `bufio.Reader` (pinned by a CH-MSGSOCK tier-1 case).
- **Existing nonce-only tests.** TestSocketRuntimeNonceOnlyModeAppliesNoPeerCheck and TestRuntimeOpsNonceOnlyAndEmbeddedPosturesApplyNoPeerCheck dial without a nonce and must send one after CODE-7; the tier-9 foreign-UID dials must present a valid nonce so the refusal stays attributable to the UID.
- **`checkLevel` skips.** probe.go silently skips unmapped check names; a rename must update probe.go, probe_test.go, and standard_test.go. Its existing omission of four Basic checks is pre-existing (Open).
- **Compliance package.** cmd/lenny-compliance is package main; nothing under tests/ can import a fixture from it, so do not reintroduce a shared fixture with the tier-10 case.
- **Schema tests at S10.** scope_test.go and tokens_wire_test.go validate `sessionId`-less runtime-ops frames and belong to SCHEMA-1 (S10).
- **Pod-scoped manifest fields.** `agent_interface`, `min_platform_version`, `mcpNonce`, sockets, and `adapterLocalTools` stay manifest fields; the proto rule and docs sweep must not retarget them.
- **Credential test fixtures.** `TestGoRuntimeSDKStartsWithNoCredentialFileAtTheManifestPath_spec_4_7` passes vacuously unless it sends a `session_start` without `credentialsPath`; mcp_test.go and runtime_test.go write flat bundles; the tier-10 `credentials_rotated` case without `sessionId` must be rewritten. Do not type `MaterializedConfig` per delivery mode.
- **SDK-warm runtime.** SDK-warm pods run the embedded `InProcessRuntime`, so CODE-9 item 6's fan-out is on that path too; no SDK-warm runtime runs over a socket.
- **Spec placeholder versus code.** The spec has a §4.7.9 step-3 placeholder manifest carrying `mcpNonce`; the code writes none and starts the accept loop after the first manifest write. Do not file an empty-nonce bypass against the spec.
- **Harness wait constraint.** CODE-6 item 1 is the one specification of the harness's `session_started` read: the `readSessionStarted` helper, the `sessionStartedWait` bound of 10 s, the widened process context, the expiry outcome, and the stub test. Every Full check in `cmd/lenny-compliance/full.go` runs its runtime under a fixed 10 s process context with 3 s `waitConn` and reads, so a 10 s wait does not fit inside it; CODE-6 item 1 makes each Full check's context the wait plus the former 10 s. The 10 s wait is no shorter than any default CODE-9 item 2 allows (below the 10 s `ConfigureWorkspace` timeout), so S16 needs no knowledge of S18's value. Do not restate the bound relative to the check's deadline, shrink the context back to 10 s, or turn the wait into a flag ([redesign.9.harness-read.1]).
- **No harness failure category.** `lenny-compliance`'s `Check` holds name, spec, pass, detail, and duration, and no §15.4.6 failure category is implemented. CODE-6 item 1's expiry reports `Pass=false` with no category; do not reintroduce a `timeout` category in CODE-6 or its tests. The [non-spec.16.review-citations.1] FACT that the category CODE-6 item 1 named is §15.4.6 is moot ([non-spec.18.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.18.fix-design-G1.1] CORRECTS).
- **Do not refile: spec wording.** §24.18 and §15.4.3 "zero Lenny knowledge", spec/23 "requires only stdin/stdout JSON Lines", §15.7 Binary protocol stdin wording, §29.2 steps 25 and 26c, the §15.4.3 v2 `lenny_nonce` migration line, §7.4 `FilesUpdated` (adapter-side), `files_updated` being new to spec, the CH-MSGSOCK **Addressing.** lead, S1/S2 forward references to S3 labels, and the §18.2 battery name all stay true or are pre-existing.
- **Do not refile: mechanisms.** SPEC-8 redial without backoff, unbounded `session_end` writes (write deadlines are a non-goal), the embedded pipe stall, the pre-auth `lc.enc` binding, the pod-wide in-flight rotation gate, `SessionUsageMeter` never deleting, post-flush token loss, the `TerminationReason` divergence, the DemoteSDK one-holder reading, Shutdown and open-sequence `session_end` exclusivity, and failed cleanup acts held for the pod's life were all weighed and declined.
- **Do not refile: docs-only.** Docs-only staleness (docs/api/internal.md, SDK docstrings, the token-usage-anomaly runbook cause, the adapter-contract.md frame list, the adapter-contract.md and build-a-runtime.md Basic traces, the adapter-contract.md exit-0 completion sentence and `session_complete` reason row, `sessionId`-less CH-RUNTIMEOPS examples, and the slo-startup-latency runbook) was refuted on materiality in every non-spec round; do not refile without a tier-11 pin ([non-spec.1.review-edit-sites.1], [non-spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1], [non-spec.7.review-docs-alignment.1]).
- **Do not refile: below the bar.** `Output` subscriptions living only for the request ctx, CODE-4 item 8's dead top-level bundle fields, the full.go fake manifest still writing per-session fields, `pkg/adapter/attach.go` left in CODE-9 Targets and the summary row, S17 and S19 reaching S3 only transitively, the pre-`Begin` checkpoint gate error returning before `barrier.link`, a subscription opened before step a, and the stall window between subscribing and step c's write ([non-spec.2.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.4.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.4.review-performance.1], [non-spec.7.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.7.review-reliability.1]).
- **Withdrawn: per-session setup at `session_start`.** MISTAKE (second spec loop, round 1): the former SPEC-3 Edit 15 retargeted §5.1/§5.2 per-session setup from "first message" to `session_start`, which put deployer setup inside the bounded `session_started` wait and inside the §6.3 agent_session_start phase, so slow setup failed every start. It was deleted. Only context creation (credential file, LLM client) belongs before `session_started` (Inbound `session_start` rule 2); do not reinstate a setup retarget to `session_start` or `OnCreate`.
- **Inbound rule 1 carries no `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` sentence.** MISTAKE: [prune.1] missed an unconditional copy of the ordering at the end of Inbound `session_start` rule 1 (written before the not-awaiting branch existed) because it was phrased from the CH-MSGSOCK side. Do not re-add a `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` sentence there, as a pointer or with the exception copied in. When a branch is added to a rule's home, grep the staged spec for every unconditional phrasing ("follows the adapter's read", "only after it has read").
- **Python and TS `CreateRequest` mirror.** spec/15's paragraph after the Reply type binds the Python and TS `CreateRequest` to the Go field set, so SPEC-5 Edit 6 (ExperimentContext, TracingContext, LLM) and Edit 1 (`OnTerminate` gains `sessionID`) oblige CODE-5 to mirror both. A non-spec-loop check; unverified ([spec.1.review-client-surface.1]). [non-spec.4.review-client-surface.1] and [non-spec.7.review-client-surface.1] did not file it, because CODE-5 opens "Mirror CODE-4" and the per-SDK TEST-1 rows reach it.
- **Step d token-only comparison.** CODE-1 item 1 step d keeps a token-only comparison at the second confirmation, while SPEC-3 Edit 13 says "A comparison of tokens alone does not conform". Serialization plus the reclaim hold make them equivalent, but a literal code reviewer will flag it. Not filed ([spec.1.review-mechanism.1]).
- **"Before the confirmation" wording.** The **Session frame writes.** row "fails after the runtime is made live and before the confirmation, because its `session_start` write fails" means "before the record": the open sequence writes after the first confirmation. The outcome (None, no record) is the same either way. Not filed ([spec.1.review-mechanism.1], [spec.1.review-fresh.1]).
- **Spec-loop lens scope.** In the spec loop a docs-alignment lens has one in-scope finding kind (an Edge cases row whose outcome reaches no staged spec text); the claim-register rule lives in tests/claim-map.json and belongs to the non-spec loop; the CODE-9 gate concurrency belongs to the non-spec loop ([spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1], [spec.1.review-plan-conformance.1], [spec.1.review-fresh.1]).
- **Do not refile: round-1 spec items.** The §28.6 CH-MSGSOCK failure-matrix row omitting the acknowledgement-timeout start failure (the matrix records transport failures); §28.8's CH-MSGSOCK row and the card's **Degradation.** bullet (the field covers an absent peer and transport failure only); the S3-to-S4 forward reference from SPEC-3 Edit 15 rule 3 to SPEC-4 Edit 2; the registry-critical-section paragraph at §4.7.1 ("the resolve, the confirmation and the record that rule 8 states"); §4.7.6 **Forward compatibility** versus deleting manifest fields at `version` 1; the who-acknowledges clauses that cite Outbound rule 1; the **Session frame writes.** intro trigger list; and plan §10.6's stale manifest-ordering sentence.
- **Do not regrow pruned rows or items.** Do not re-add helper names or a resolution route to CODE-9 item 3, and do not regrow the TEST-1 Go SDK row with clauses that restate CODE-4; a new SDK behavior goes into a CODE-4 item and the row's per-item rule covers it ([prune.3]). Do not regrow TEST-1 row 3 with a clause per transition, branch, caller, or fixture: a new CODE-9 rule is covered by the row's per-rule sentence, and a new fixture reason goes into Trap **Gate test fixtures** ([prune.4]).
- **0084 row labels.** 0084's token fold is its CODE-2 and its MCP providers are its CODE-3; the impact row names neither by number. SPEC-1 Edit 7 removing 0079's DRAINING clause does not contradict 0079.

- **Failed transition sites.** The failed transition on an acknowledgement failure is stated in CODE-9 item 1 Read (errored match), item 1 Failed (every non-success return), and item 2 (failed before `writeSessionEnd`). They agree; item 2 adds only the ordering. Edit one, check the other two ([non-spec.1.review-single-source.1]).
- **No second gate writer.** MISTAKE: an earlier round made both the open sequence and the Attach filter write read, which opened a late-ack race. Do not reintroduce the Attach filter as a gate writer or a Failed-from-read rule ([non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]).
- **No predicate field.** Do not reintroduce a handshake predicate field on `Server` or a main.go injection helper; `rotationgate.NewPodAdapter` never received one and its Servers silently never waited ([non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]).
- **`DialPeer` returns early.** `rotationgate.DialPeer` returns right after writing `lifecycle_support`, before the adapter processes it, so a start issued at once does not wait. Block on `s.Lifecycle.WaitHandshake` with a positive wait first ([non-spec.1.fix-G2.2], [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]).
- **Unconditional subscribe.** Do not reintroduce a subscription opened before step c on every start, or a `waitCtx` that outlives the sequence ([non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]).
- **New `Server` timeout fields.** State the zero-value meaning on the field; a flag-only default leaves non-binary Servers at zero ([non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]).
- **Prepended frames de-pin tests.** A test that keeps passing after a deliverable prepends a frame is not "broken" and escapes the per-deliverable update rule; check line-indexed captures whenever a deliverable prepends a frame ([non-spec.7.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.7.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **`readResponse` shares the scanner.** `SubprocessExecutor.readResponse` scans the same `sess.stdout`; a CODE-9 item 6 fan-out reader started at spawn rather than at the first `Output` would race the developer-loop `Send` path ([non-spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]).
- **Tier-3 wire-order transport.** The TEST-1 "Wire order on one runtime connection" case needs a CODE-7 inventory row if it dials `CH-MSGSOCK`, and none over stdin ([non-spec.4.review-applicability.1]).
- **Rejected gate alternatives.** Making `subscriber.send` drop on full (changes Attach delivery), shortening the wait to bound the stall, naming a wake-all primitive in CODE-9 item 1 (synchronization stays IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE), and making teardown take `s.ops` were weighed and rejected ([non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.7.fix-design-G2.1], [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]).

## Ledger

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-3 Edit 5 **Inbound: `session_end`** rule 2 now exempts, besides the late `llm_request_completed`, "the `session_started` that **Outbound: `session_started`** obliges for a `session_start` read before the `session_end`" — BECAUSE the ack-timeout row of **Session frame writes.** writes `session_end` while the runtime may still be creating the context, and rule 2's ban collided with Outbound rule 1's obligation; permitting the late frame matches CODE-4 item 2, CODE-5 item 3, and the SPEC-5 Edit 1 Handler comment, and Outbound rule 5 already drops it by `startId` — ALTERNATIVES: forbidding the late ack (exception in Outbound rule 1 plus SDK suppression work for a frame nobody reads); deleting rule 2's no-further-frame clause (breaks CODE-4 item 4's ended mark and the SPEC-6 session lifetime row); a predicate like "a frame that completes work begun before the session_end" (would admit checkpoint_ready, interrupt_acknowledged, and credentials_acknowledged, contradicting the Edge-cases residual).
WATCHOUT: rule 2 points at **Outbound: `session_started`** for when the late ack is written; do not restate rule 1's "after it has created the session's context" condition in rule 2, and do not add a matching exception to Outbound rule 1 — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 5 rule 2 and Edit 15 rule 1.
FACT: CODE-4 item 4's ended mark covers only `response` and `tool_call`, so it stays conformant with widened rule 2 and needs no edit — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 4.
CORRECTS [standing context Open: CODE-4 item 2 `session_started`-after-`session_end` divergence]: closed by this edit; the spec now permits the late `session_started`, so CODE-4 item 2 is conformant as staged.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: resolve the session_started-vs-session_end collision by exempting, in SPEC-3 Edit 5 **Inbound: `session_end`** rule 2, "the `session_started` that **Outbound: `session_started`** obliges for a `session_start` read before the `session_end`" — a pointer to the frame's own home, not a restated condition. The runtime keeps writing the acknowledgement; the adapter drops it by `startId` (Outbound `session_started` rule 5) — BECAUSE it makes CODE-4 item 2, CODE-5 item 3, and the SPEC-5 Edit 1 Handler comment conformant as staged, with no non-spec cascade, and rule 1 stays the one home for when the frame is written — ALTERNATIVES: forbid the late ack (add an exception to Outbound rule 1 and extend CODE-4 item 4's ended mark to `session_started`): costs SDK work and conformance surface for a frame the adapter already discards; delete rule 2's "no further frame" clause: withdraws the response/tool_call ban that CODE-4 item 4 and the SPEC-6 **session lifetime** row rely on; a predicate such as "frames answering work begun before session_end": would also admit `checkpoint_ready`/`interrupt_acknowledged`, which changes semantics.
CORRECTS [Standing context Open, "`session_started` after `session_end` during `OnCreate`"]: once this fix lands that divergence is closed in the spec, not by a CODE-4 change. CODE-4 item 2's write is conformant, and item 4's ended mark rightly stays limited to `response` and `tool_call`. Drop the Open entry.
WATCHOUT: Standing context Traps "Withdrawn: fail the pod on missed ack and ack counting" says "rule 1 does not oblige an ack after `session_end`". After this fix, rule 1 does oblige the ack for a start read before the `session_end`, and rule 2 permits it. That clause was never the reason the option was withdrawn (the shared bind token and co-tenant kill were), so do not reopen ack counting because of it. — EVIDENCE: review-log.md Standing context Traps; spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 15 rule 1

### [spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: Round-2 diff to spec-changes.md: SPEC-3 Edit 15 (§5.1/§5.2 setup retarget) deleted and old Edit 16 renumbered to Edit 15; session_start rule 1 lost its CH-RUNTIMEOPS sentences; session_start rule 3 now defines "hold" (from session_start read, incl. creation, to session_end read); session_end rule 2 releases a context still being created once creation ends; SPEC-4 Edit 2 adds an adapter-chosen deferral bound. No stale "Edit 16" or §5.1 reference remains in the proposal. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:226, :251, :342, :378
WATCHOUT: With session_end now readable during context creation, Outbound session_started rule 1 (write after creation), rule 2 (error after failed creation), and SPEC-5 Edit 1's Handler comment ("writes the session's session_started once OnCreate returns") oblige a frame that session_end rule 2 forbids ("writes no further frame addressed to the session other than llm_request_completed"). Filed. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:251, :353-354, :428
FACT: files_updated is sent only on the mid-session FinalizeWorkspace path, so a gate in "not started" never holds that sender pre-start. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go:314

### [spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: The round-2 spec diff is confined to SPEC-3 (Edit 15 §5.1/§5.2 retarget deleted, old Edit 16 renumbered to Edit 15, Inbound session_start rules 1 and 3, Inbound session_end rule 2) and SPEC-4 Edit 2 (deferral bound). No stale "Edit 16" or §5.1 reference remains in the proposal directory. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:177, spec-changes.md:342
FACT: session_start, session_started, and session_end are intra-pod frames. They add no etcd, Postgres, or Redis writes, and the slot serialization is per slot identifier, so the open sequence's bounded wait costs co-tenants on a concurrent pool nothing at the spec level. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:320 (Edit 12)
WATCHOUT: Inbound session_start rule 3 now counts a session as held while its context is being created. After an acknowledgement timeout, session_end and then a retry's session_start can arrive while the first creation is still running. That gives two creations for one sessionId. The adapter drops the stale session_started by startId (Outbound rule 5). Keeping the two contexts apart is left to the runtime SDKs. I judged this below the bar. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:225, spec-changes.md:248, spec-changes.md:358

### [spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: Round-2 spec diff is six hunks: SPEC-3 header drops §5.1, Inbound session_start rule 1 loses the CH-RUNTIMEOPS sentences, rule 3 defines "holds" as read-of-start (including creation) to read-of-end, session_end rule 2 defers release until creation ends, old Edit 15 (§5.1/§5.2 setup retarget) deleted and Edit 16 renumbered to 15, SPEC-4 Edit 2 adds a deferral bound. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:177, :224-226, :251, :342, :378
WATCHOUT: With "holds" now covering context creation, Outbound session_started rule 1 ("writes it again for a session_start that names a session it already holds") would acknowledge a duplicate start before creation ends.
