### [spec.4.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens — BECAUSE the staged spec edits add no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write (per start: two intra-pod CH-MSGSOCK frames plus one session_end), the slot serialization is keyed per slot identifier so co-tenants never queue behind another session's acknowledgement wait, and every wait the spec stages is bounded (rule 3 bound plus request deadline; §5.2 Edit 12 bound for cleanups) — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing §6.3 "Still on hot path (SDK-warm)" ("session start is already done") as a missed edit site now that SDK-warm ConfigureWorkspace writes session_start and waits for session_started; not filed because §6.3 means the pre-connected SDK session, which SPEC-7 Edit 3 also keeps ("the session is already connected"), and the code already counts ConfigureWorkspace as the agent session start phase.
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess accepts one CH-MSGSOCK connection for the pod's life and marks it ended sticky, so a dropped connection mid-ack-wait cannot be followed by a reconnect that would carry a session with no session_start. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:324-349, 388-398
FACT: a failed act under §5.2 holds the slot identifier for the pod's life (and enters `leaked` for a running slot whose runtime close fails), so an open sequence that outlasts a cleanup's slot-serialization bound costs the identifier, not correctness; the CODE-9 item 2 default constraint (below the 10 s window with room for session_end) is what keeps the hold-timeout path off it. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:464-473; non-spec-changes.md:212
WATCHOUT: for an awaiting session the record (running) is taken only after session_started is read, so the SPEC-4 Edit 2 deferral practically bites only on callers holding a superseded attempt's slotState or on not-awaiting sessions; do not file "unbounded co-tenant cost" on the gate without a path that reaches a pending gate after running. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Session frame writes paragraph and Outbound session_started rule 3

### [spec.6.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no finding filed on the new SPEC-3 Edit 16 (§6.3 SDK-warm hot path) — BECAUSE both anchors exist verbatim (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:334 "(session start is already done)", :347 "(elimination of agent session start time)"), the replacement cites rule 3 rather than restating it, and no other spec site claims SDK-warm eliminates session start — ALTERNATIVES: filing that the SDK-warm ack wait inside ConfigureWorkspace may fall in the excluded "workspace materialization" span of lenny_session_startup_duration_seconds (spec/16_observability.md:19); rejected as a measurement-scope ambiguity, not a defect.
FACT: the §10.1 coordinatorHoldTimeoutSeconds default is 120 s (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:58), so a hold-timeout termination cannot plausibly meet a pending open sequence whose session_started wait is bounded below 10 s; the §5.2 failed-act path (slot held for pod life) from a serialization wait outlasting the 10 s graceful window is not reachable at default flags — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:478, :466-474
FACT: the staged spec adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write; per-start cost is three intra-pod frames (session_start, session_started, session_end) and one per-slot (not pod-wide) serialization, so top-tier write-rate math is zero for the control plane.
WATCHOUT: a deployer OnCreate that runs longer than the adapter's ack bound now fails every start (and on SDK-warm fails the pod via the §4.7.1 ConfigureWorkspace row); this is the accepted consequence of the owner's "session_started once context exists" decision plus SPEC-5 Edit 1, warned in DOCS-1 item 4. Do not refile as a spec defect. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5 Edit 1 ("writes the session's `session_started` once OnCreate returns")
USEFUL [Settled "Gateway Shutdown budgets"]: saved re-deriving the Shutdown-vs-pending-wait budget race.

### [spec.6.review-reliability.1]

An operator who raises the flag above 10 s makes a hold-timeout termination racing a pending start fail closed, which holds the slot for the pod's life. This is a misconfiguration degradation, it fails closed, and it was not filed. ### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the open sequence's wait (CODE-9 item 2) is the only writer that moves the ack gate out of pending; the Attach filter (CODE-1 item 4) consumes `session_started` and writes no gate state — BECAUSE a second writer let a late ack, read by a co-tenant's Attach stream after waitCtx expired, move the gate to read and release a sender before item 2 recorded failed, violating SPEC-4 Edit 2; the sequence's own Output subscription already sees every frame through fanOut (pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go fanOut/broadcast) — ALTERNATIVES: monotonic Failed (does not close the window), a context callback failing the gate at waitCtx end (adds a trigger and an ordering).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce the Attach filter as a gate writer, or a Failed-from-read rule; with one writer, read-to-failed is unreachable and the Failed bullet's "unless already released" guard suffices — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-9 item 1 Read and Failed bullets; pkg/adapter/attach.go demuxSessionOutput delivers unscoped frames to every Attach stream.
MISTAKE: an earlier round made both the open sequence and the Attach filter write the gate's read transition ("whichever reader comes second finds it done"); this opened a late-ack race and cost a non-spec round 1 finding.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: CODE-9 item 2 reads handshake completion from `s.Lifecycle.WaitHandshake(ctx, 0)` evaluated as the open sequence begins; the injected predicate, its Server field, the main.go helper, the TEST-1 main_test.go row and its CODE-7 inventory dial row are deleted — BECAUSE `Server.Lifecycle` is already the *RuntimeOps and a non-positive wait reports state without blocking, and both Lifecycle wiring sites (cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go, rotationgate.NewPodAdapter) already set it — ALTERNATIVES: an exported predicate field that rotationgate also sets (duplicates Lifecycle; every future Server builder must remember it).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce a handshake predicate field on Server or a main.go injection helper; rotationgate.NewPodAdapter never received it and its Servers silently never waited. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/rotationgate/rotationgate.go `s.Lifecycle = lc`; pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go `WaitHandshake`
FACT: `WaitHandshake(ctx, wait<=0)` checks the current handshake state without blocking. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go WaitHandshake doc comment
CORRECTS [non-spec.9.fix-G2.1]: Standing context's note that the `cmd/lenny-adapter/main_test.go` predicate dial has its own CODE-7 inventory row is false; the row and the TEST-1 case it served are deleted.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.2]
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 row `TestSessionFramesStayPairedAcrossReclaimAndRetry_spec_28_5_3` must issue no SignalDeadline against attempt 1. A frame written under attempt 1's acknowledgement may be read during attempt 2's pending `session_started` (the accepted Edge-cases residual, and CODE-9 item 3's nil-return check is not atomic with the write), so an attempt-1 signal makes the ordering assertion flaky by construction. The row now signals only after attempt 2's bind, concurrent with attempt 2's StartSession, and asserts against attempt 2's `session_started`. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edge-cases residual; non-spec-changes.md CODE-9 item 3
WATCHOUT: the row's precondition that `s.Lifecycle.WaitHandshake` reports the DialPeer handshake before attempt 2's StartSession is a trap, not plumbing: DialPeer returns before the adapter processes lifecycle_support, and without it the start does not wait and the assertion fails.

CORRECTS [non-spec.10.follow-up-fix.1]: same as above.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: Checkpoint and a clean Interrupt wait on the gate before `s.ops.Begin` and re-check the same entry after `Begin` returns, before `RequestCheckpoint` / `interruptViaLifecycle`, with a non-waiting call that treats not started or pending as an error (CODE-9 item 3 caller bullets) — BECAUSE `s.ops.Begin` can queue behind a co-tenant's upload while Shutdown or hold-timeout pass 1 releases the entry and a successor attempt registers under the same id; without the re-check the predecessor's frame reaches the runtime holding the successor before its session_started — ALTERNATIVES: waiting after Begin (forbidden by owner decision 2026-10-04); making teardown take s.ops (new lock interaction).
WATCHOUT: Do not justify the post-Begin call as "cannot wait because a read or not-awaiting gate moves only to failed or released": Resume resets the same entry to pending. The non-waiting property is a stated constraint of the call, not a derived property of the gate — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-9 item 1 Reset; review-log Trap "Do not reinstate moves only to released".
WATCHOUT: The TEST-1 queued-in-Begin subtest discriminates only with a successor registered under the same id before Begin returns (same reason as "Released-branch subtest needs a successor") — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:111 Begin, :163 RequestCheckpoint; pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go:44 Begin, :58 interruptViaLifecycle.
FACT: Interrupt takes the pod-wide `s.ops.Begin(ctx, opInterrupt, "")` before branching; FinalizeWorkspace holds the slot guard via `defer releaseGuard()` across `SignalFilesUpdated` — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go:44; pkg/adapter/staging.go:219-224, :315. TEST-1's per-caller lock-placement subtests pin both restructurings.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the open sequence's wait (CODE-9 item 2) is the gate's only writer of read; the Attach filter (CODE-1 item 4) consumes every session_started and touches no gate — BECAUSE with one writer the sequence alone ends pending, as read on the matching error-free frame or as failed when its wait ends, so a late ack can no longer release a waiter before the start's failure is recorded; the open sequence's own Output subscription already receives every frame through the fan-out — ALTERNATIVES: keep two writers and make Failed run only from pending/not started (rejected: a late ack read by Attach still releases a sender before the sequence learns of its own timeout); have the sequence fail the gate before its waitCtx expires (rejected: needs a second deadline, hair).
DECISION: Failed's "unless the gate is already released" guard is kept unchanged — BECAUSE with a single writer read->failed is unreachable: the only non-(true,nil) return after a read is step e, which runs only when the entry was removed, and every removal releases the gate in deregisterSlotLocked (CODE-9 item 1 Released) — ALTERNATIVES: rewrite as "runs only from not started or pending" (same length, but changes nothing an implementor builds).
WATCHOUT: the CODE-9 item 1 sentence "The filter drops every other session_started" must go with the writer, because after the fix the Attach filter drops matching frames too; CODE-1 item 4 is the one home of the filter's behaviour — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:45, :208

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: Checkpoint and clean Interrupt wait on the gate before `s.ops.Begin` and re-check the same entry after `Begin` returns with a call that does not wait (a not-started or pending gate there counts as an error) — BECAUSE `s.ops.Begin` can queue behind a co-tenant's whole upload while Shutdown or hold-timeout pass 1 releases the entry and a successor attempt starts; without the re-check the predecessor's `checkpoint_request`/`interrupt_request` precedes the successor's `session_started` (SPEC-4 Edit 2) — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's justification "a read or not-awaiting gate moves only to failed or released" was rejected because a later start on the same entry (Resume claims the existing entry) can reset it to pending, and Standing context already bans "moves only to" claims; a waiting post-Begin call was rejected because it waits under `s.ops`; waiting only after Begin was rejected by the owner decision.
WATCHOUT: the post-`Begin` re-check is the discriminating assertion for this window only when the test releases the entry while the call is queued in `Begin` (hold a co-tenant checkpoint in its upload); a pre-Begin-only implementation writes the frame there. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:111, :163; pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go:44, :56-57
CORRECTS [prune.2]: "the calls behind s.ops.Begin return at once" stopped holding once prune.2 left only the pre-Begin call; review-log Standing context "Guard holders and the gate" said "call the gate before s.ops.Begin, never under the pod-level op lock", which is now true only of the waiting call.

FACT: CODE-9 item 1 lets the gate leave read for failed. On a concurrent pool a co-tenant's Attach stream sees every session_started, because demuxSessionOutput passes frames of unscoped types to every stream. A late ack can therefore release a waiting sender after the wait has already ended. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:208-209, :212; pkg/adapter/attach.go:70, :318

### [non-spec.1.review-fresh.1]
- DECISION: filed one finding: TEST-1 row 2's ordering clause ("no `deadline_approaching` reaches the peer before ... the `session_started` carrying the current attempt's `startId`") asserts an ordering the design does not give — BECAUSE the spec-changes Edge-cases residual (spec-changes.md:25) lets a frame written under attempt 1's acknowledgement be read after attempt 2's `session_start`, and CODE-9 item 3's nil return on a read gate is check-then-write, so a sender that passed attempt 1's gate can write after attempt 1's release and attempt 2's `session_start`; row 2's interleavings reach both. This settles the Open "Row 2 ordering key" entry against the staged clause — ALTERNATIVES: a production-level design finding on the check-then-write window (rejected: needs a gateway sender for a session it is compensating, and the only strict remedy is writing under the gate's leaf lock across blocking I/O, which the "Rollback under lock" trap forbids).
- FACT: The CODE-9 gate examined as a concurrency mechanism holds: a woken waiter re-reads state, so a read-then-released race returns an error rather than a frame; Read runs only from pending, so the Attach filter and the open-sequence wait cannot double-transition; deregisterSlotLocked is the sole removal; every sender caller's ctx is bounded (SignalDeadline additionally by the gateway's interruptDeadline at pkg/gateway/sessionserver/expiry_warning.go:61). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:204-226; pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:556-580
- FACT: `SocketRuntimeProcess` subscriber `send` blocks the shared reader when a subscriber's 64-line feed is full and it is not done, so any unread `Output` subscription stalls every co-tenant's output; CODE-9 item 2's wait subscription is safe only because it is read continuously or cancelled at return. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:166-203, :475-497
- FACT: The handshake predicate CODE-9 item 2 needs already exists as `RuntimeOps.WaitHandshake(ctx, 0)`, and `resetConn` re-arms it per connection. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:266-304, :555-580
- FACT: SPEC-3 Edit 16 (§6.3) anchors resolve at spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:334 and :347; the review log's Settled line "SPEC-3 has no Edit 16" is now stale. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:367
- CORRECTS [Standing context Settled "No setup retarget"]: "(SPEC-3 has no Edit 16)" is false since SPEC-3 Edit 16 (§6.3 SDK-warm hot path) was added; Edit 15 is still the `session_started` section.

### [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: returned no findings after tracing the CODE-9 acknowledgement gate as a concurrency mechanism (state transitions, waiter wakeups, Attach filter vs open-sequence read, bounds on every sender, connection drop mid-wait, co-tenant cost) — BECAUSE every path traced closes: every entry removal goes through deregisterSlotLocked (so Released always fires), every session_end is preceded by the gate leaving read, and every waiter's ctx is bounded — ALTERNATIVES: reporting the first-session "not awaiting" residual (below) was rejected because SPEC-3 Outbound session_started rule 3 sanctions it explicitly.
FACT: the only delete from s.slots is deregisterSlotLocked, reached only through reclaimSlotLocked (callers session.go:320, slotsession.go:278/341/540), so every removal also opens the reclaim hold; this is what makes CODE-1 step d's token-only check sound for untokened StartSession/ConfigureWorkspace entries — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:204-256
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess.Output gives every subscriber every frame (no per-session filter) and broadcast sends sequentially with a blocking send into a 64-deep feed, so an unread subscription stalls the shared reader for all co-tenants; the open-sequence wait must drain its subscription and cancel it (defer cancel covers the non-waiting start) — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:166-203, 405-418, 475-499
FACT: RuntimeOps.resetConn re-arms the ready channel on a CH-RUNTIMEOPS drop, so the handshake predicate (WaitHandshake(ctx,0)) reports false again after a drop until the runtime re-handshakes — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go:269-288, 561-580
WATCHOUT: the adapter never waits for the CH-RUNTIMEOPS handshake before StartSession's open sequence (the gateway probes the level after StartSession via WaitHandshake), and the Go SDK dials CH-MSGSOCK before it dials CH-RUNTIMEOPS, so on a pod-warm pod's first session the rule-3 predicate is usually false and that session is "not awaiting" for its life. This is the rule-3 residual, not a defect, but CODE-4 item 9's rationale ("the adapter writes a session's events only after it reads session_started") does not hold for such a session — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/integrationlevel.go:48-56, sdks/runtime/go/runtime/runtime.go:222-245, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindlaunch.go:62-86
WATCHOUT: the gateway does not call DemoteSDK after a ConfigureWorkspace failure in code; it reclaims the pod (bindlaunch.go reclaim). CODE-1 item 2's "the gateway's DemoteSDK fallback then finds no session running" describes the §4.7.1 ConfigureWorkspace row rather than the tree — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindlaunch.go:62-66, spec/04_system-components.md:673
UNVERIFIED: CODE-9 item 2 injects the handshake predicate only from cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go; tests that build a Server directly (rotationgate.NewPodAdapter, pkg/adapter tier-1 tests) must set it themselves or the gate never engages. Server.Lifecycle is already *RuntimeOps with WaitHandshake, so the implementor may derive it instead — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/server.go:244

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens, after examining the CODE-9 gate as a concurrency mechanism — BECAUSE the gate is in-memory on the slotState (no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write; nothing for a Postgres failover or Redis reset to lose), each slot entry sees exactly one start (slotState.started refuses a second), so "a waiter from before a start waits for that start's outcome" holds, a pending gate always leaves pending within SessionStartAckTimeout (read or failed) or on deregistration (released), and the open-sequence wait holds only the per-slot guard, never s.ops or s.mu — ALTERNATIVES: a finding on the unstated closed-subscription arm of the item 2 wait (rejected: the outcome is a bounded start failure, which is no worse than shipped), on interrupt's gate wait plus RequestInterrupt each bounded by deadlineMs (rejected: the gate is pending only during a start, which is rare and bounded), and on the non-waiting branch's subscription that is never read (rejected: a deferred cancel at return makes the window microseconds).
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess.broadcast sends to each subscriber sequentially, and subscriber.send blocks while that subscriber's 64-slot feed is full and it is not done. Despite the code comment, one subscriber that is registered and unread therefore stalls the fan-out for every co-tenant after 64 frames. Any new Output subscription (the CODE-9 item 2 wait) must be read or cancelled promptly. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:198-203, 405-415
FACT: the hold-timeout termination runs CloseListener (pod-scope teardown) before pass 1, so a pending open-sequence wait sees its subscription channel close rather than waiting out its timer, provided the wait treats a closed channel as "ended without the frame". EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:241-249
FACT: deregisterSlotLocked is the only delete from s.slots, so the CODE-9 item 1 Released transition does cover every removal. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:204-213
FACT: the gate wait does not reach the pod-level s.ops lock: Checkpoint resolves its slot pointer via checkpointRootsForSession before s.ops.Begin, and Interrupt's clean-branch condition (s.Lifecycle.Supports("interrupt")) can be evaluated before s.ops.Begin. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:93-111, pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go:44-58

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]

FACT: `SocketRuntimeProcess.acceptLoop` in the tree accepts exactly once and installs whatever connects first; CODE-7 item 3's loop-on-failure is what closes the nonce-only first-dialer race. `wrap` returns the bare listener in nonce-only and embedded (`NoSocketBoundary`) postures, so item 3 must run the handshake itself in every posture — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:331-350, pkg/adapter/peercred.go:135-140
FACT: the socket fan-out `subscriber.send` blocks the shared reader once a live, unread subscriber's 64-slot feed fills (`broadcast` sends sequentially). CODE-9 item 2's pre-step-c subscription is harmless only because `openRuntimeSession` returns (and cancels `waitCtx`) right after step d/e on the not-awaiting branch; any later change that keeps that subscription alive unread stalls co-tenant Attach streams — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:166-207, 405-415
FACT: no `RuntimeOps.Terminate` caller exists outside tests, so deleting `terminate` (CODE-3 item 3) removes no termination or revocation control; direct-mode revocation terminates through the gateway, not a CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame — EVIDENCE: grep of `.Terminate(` in pkg/adapter and cmd/lenny-adapter; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:82
FACT: `session_start` `llm` members carry no secret material (apiKeyEnv is a variable name; headers are the anthropic-version default), so moving them off the pod-global manifest file is stricter — EVIDENCE: proposal spec-changes.md:236-240
USEFUL [spec.1.review-security.1]: the direct-mode self-report and kept-process residual entries settled both trust-boundary questions; §11.2 still names direct-mode underreporting as the accepted residual (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:49).
DECISION: no security finding this round — BECAUSE the acknowledgement gate only gates frames to the runtime itself (a forged or withheld `session_started` harms only the forging runtime's own sessions), every gate error routes to an existing bounded failure branch, rotation rewrites the credential file before the gated frame, and no CRD, RBAC, NetworkPolicy, webhook, or store surface is touched — ALTERNATIVES: filing the 500 ms sequential handshake as an in-pod accept-loop DoS (rejected: hardening below the bar) and the Debug-level `session_end_write_failed` log as unsurfaced residual state (rejected: a failed write implies the connection is gone, and connection end ends every session under Inbound `session_end` rule 1).

### [non-spec.1.review-single-source.1]
DECISION: filed one (g) finding: the TEST-1 row 1 non-happy-path cell says "one subtest per row" of **Session frame writes.** and then, "in addition", writes out the outcomes of two of those rows again (the `DemoteSDK` deadline row and the acknowledgement-failure row) — BECAUSE a reader could implement both rules from the test cell alone, and the directive says tests are structural rules that point at homes — ALTERNATIVES: filing the per-caller lock lines in CODE-9 item 3 beside its constraint paragraph was rejected, because [prune.3] kept them on purpose and they do not disagree with the constraint; the summary's "Watch out for" ordering bullets were rejected because they are one-line summaries of deliverables.
FACT: the SocketRuntimeProcess fan-out never blocks the shared reader on one subscriber (buffered feed per subscriber, its own pump goroutine), so an open-sequence subscription that is never read (the non-waiting branch of CODE-9 item 2) costs co-tenants nothing — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:194-200, 401-405 FALSIFIED [prune.4]: a full feed blocks the shared reader and so every sibling (Settled **Socket fan-out stall**).

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `Server.SessionStartAckTimeout` resolves zero to a compiled default on the field itself, which the `--session-start-ack-timeout` flag defaults to and overrides (CODE-9 item 2) — BECAUSE every `adapter.New` caller other than main.go (rotationgate.NewPodAdapter, pkg/adapter test Servers, TEST-1 rows) leaves the field zero, and "earlier of the deadline and 0" fails every wait at once — ALTERNATIVES: setting it in each test Server (misses other callers); restoring the main.go helper (pins the binary only); zero as unbounded (breaks item 3's every-waiter-has-a-deadline rule).
WATCHOUT: a new adapter `Server` timeout field must state its zero-value meaning on the field, as CredentialsAckTimeout and HeartbeatAckTimeout do; a flag-only default leaves non-binary Servers at zero — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/server.go CredentialsAckTimeout and HeartbeatAckTimeout doc comments; tests/testinfra/rotationgate/rotationgate.go NewPodAdapter sets no SessionStartAckTimeout.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-9 item 2 states that a zero `Server.SessionStartAckTimeout` selects the compiled default, and that the `--session-start-ack-timeout` flag defaults to that value and overrides it. The replacement is in place and about the same length, and item 2 is the field's one home. BECAUSE `adapter.New` callers (`rotationgate.NewPodAdapter` and every pkg/adapter test Server) never set the field, and every other adapter timeout field already treats zero as "use the default". ALTERNATIVES: (a) set the field in `rotationgate.NewPodAdapter` and in the tests. Rejected because every unwired caller would still be broken. (b) restore the main.go wiring helper and its main_test row. Rejected because it pins only the binary, and the flag can only re-supply a value the field already defaults to. (c) treat zero as unbounded. Rejected because it breaks item 3's rule that every waiter carries a deadline.
FACT: adapter timeout fields resolve zero at the point of use, for example CredentialsAckTimeout in pkg/adapter/credentials.go (the zero check near line 200). DefaultDemoteTimeout (pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:27) is the precedent for a package constant that the flag and the field share. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/server.go:256-303
WATCHOUT: item 3 cites `SessionStartAckTimeout` by name for FinalizeWorkspace and for zero-bound callers. Each of those sites means the field's effective value after the zero default. Do not add a second statement of the default there.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-9 item 2 decides wait-or-not first (s.Lifecycle set and WaitHandshake(ctx, 0) true as the sequence begins), subscribes to s.Runtime.Output only on a waiting start, and cancels waitCtx on every return — BECAUSE an unread subscription on SocketRuntimeProcess blocks the shared fan-out after its 64-slot feed fills, stalling co-tenant Attach streams (incl. heartbeat_ack) up to SessionStartAckTimeout; cancelling on every return also covers a step-c write failure — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's literal prefix/append (left predicate after the subscription, cancel hidden in the read branch); non-blocking subscriber.send (drops frames for every subscriber, out of scope); shorter ack timeout (same defect, smaller).
WATCHOUT: the "non-waiting branch subscription is harmless because a deferred cancel makes the window microseconds" rejection (review-log entry at old line 547) was wrong as staged text: item 2 never said the cancel covered the non-waiting branch, and waitCtx outlived the call. Do not reintroduce an unconditional subscribe before step c. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go subscriber.send / broadcast / Output (ctx.Done unsubscribe)
CORRECTS [547 DECISION, perf lens]: the non-waiting-branch subscription was a real defect; the staged text stated no deferred cancel.
USEFUL [526/540/548 FACT]: subscriber.send blocks on a full feed despite its doc comment; saved re-deriving the stall.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-9 item 2 decides whether the start waits once, first, and only a waiting start opens the `Output` subscription; the sequence cancels `waitCtx` on every return (including a step-c write failure) — BECAUSE the subscription's lifetime must equal the wait's, and an unread subscriber stalls the shared reader — ALTERNATIVES: bounding the stall by shortening the wait (same defect, smaller); making `subscriber.send` non-blocking/drop-on-full (changes CH-MSGSOCK delivery for Attach, out of scope, loses frames).
FACT: `SocketRuntimeProcess.broadcast` sends to each subscriber sequentially and `subscriber.send` blocks on a full 64-slot feed, so one unread subscriber stalls every co-tenant's Attach stream after ~65 frames; the doc comments on `send`/`broadcast` claim otherwise and are wrong. `Output` ignores its sessionID argument, so a wait subscription receives every session's frames. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:165-167, :197-202, :405-416, :474-492
WATCHOUT: item 1's Reset bullet already reads "When the start will wait (item 2)" before step c, so the wait predicate is necessarily evaluated before step c; item 2's old text placed it "after step c", which was the inconsistency that let the subscription be opened unconditionally. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-9 item 1 Reset, item 2
OPEN: TEST-1 row 3 has no subtest pinning "a non-waiting start opens no subscription / waitCtx cancelled on return"; the row-3 group should decide whether a subtest belongs there (one case, pointing at item 2).

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: TEST-1 row 3 lock-placement subtests run each CODE-9 item 3 caller against a gate that is not started (session claimed, its `StartSession` parked in `Runtime.Start`) rather than a pending one — BECAUSE a pending gate exists only inside an open sequence, which holds the per-slot guard, so "guard acquirable while pending" fails for every implementation; the not-started window is the one state where a waiter under the guard would deadlock the start, so it is the discriminating fixture — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the guard half (rejected: leaves FinalizeWorkspace's release-before-wait constraint unpinned); deleting the subtest family (rejected: item 3's lock constraint then has no test).
FACT: `StartSession` takes no slot guard until `openRuntimeSession` step a; `Runtime.Start` runs unguarded after the claim. A mid-session `FinalizeWorkspace` (`allowStarted: true`, `allowCreate: false`) resolves an existing not-yet-started entry, so it too can reach the gate in that window — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:124-176; pkg/adapter/slot.go:132-146; pkg/adapter/staging.go:229-231.
WATCHOUT: `allowStarted` permits a started entry; it does not require one. Do not reason that mid-session RPCs only see started entries — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slot.go:146.
DECISION: zero `SessionStartAckTimeout` gets one row-3 case (delayed answer starts, withheld answer fails within item 2's bound) — BECAUSE row 2's `rotationgate` Server is zero-valued but passes vacuously under immediate expiry, and the register row reads only the flag default.
DECISION: callers' error branch gets one row-3 case per item 3 caller on a failed gate, with the RotateCredentials note "no LEASE_REJECTED" as the discriminating assertion — BECAUSE deadline-ended waits return DeadlineExceeded and reach today's timeout arms by themselves; only a non-deadline error reaches `rotateProviderFull`'s default arm (EmitLeaseRejected + codes.Internal) — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/credentials.go:211-227.
USEFUL [non-spec.10.review-mechanism.1]: the "Rotation gate error" standing-context line located the LEASE_REJECTED trap directly.

### [non-spec.4.review-fresh.1]
FACT: A pending acknowledgement gate implies the opener holds the session's per-slot guard: CODE-1 item 1 step a takes it (or Resume holds it from resume.go:58), and the CODE-9 wait sits between steps c and d. StartSession takes no guard before openRuntimeSession, so the guard is free only while the gate is `not started` (StartSession parked in Runtime.Start after its claim). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:20, :206, :211; pkg/adapter/session.go:124-176; pkg/adapter/resume.go:58,161
FACT: A mid-session FinalizeWorkspace blocks in acquireSlotGuardForResolve while an open sequence holds the guard, so it can reach awaitSessionStarted only on a `not started` gate, never on a pending one. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go:219-224, pkg/adapter/bindattempt.go:256-267, pkg/adapter/slot.go:146

FACT: Every slot registry removal is the one `delete(s.slots, ...)` in deregisterSlotLocked, so CODE-9's Released transition covers every removal. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:210
FACT: SubprocessExecutor (the --runtime-bin transport) spawns one child per session, and its Output today is documented single-consumer. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/subprocess.go:228-254, :309-341
USEFUL [Guard holders and the gate]: confirmed the gate placement audit without re-deriving it.

### [non-spec.4.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The proposal touches no CRD, status subresource, finalizer, admission webhook, controller, or apiserver path; every mechanism is intra-pod (adapter <-> runtime over CH-MSGSOCK/CH-RUNTIMEOPS). The Kubernetes-idiom lens has nothing to attach to beyond controller-on-hot-path, and no controller reconcile sits on the start path. — EVIDENCE: grep for CRD|status|controller|webhook|finalizer over spec-changes.md and non-spec-changes.md returns only unrelated "condition"/"Preconditions" hits.
FACT: FinalizeWorkspace signals files_updated only on the mid_session arm, so CODE-9 item 3's FinalizeWorkspace wait never runs against a not-started gate on the pre-start path. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/staging.go:314 `if midSession && s.Lifecycle != nil`
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess fan-out subscriber send blocks the shared reader when a subscriber's 64-slot feed is full and not done, so the open-sequence wait's subscription must drain continuously until it cancels (CODE-9 item 2 reads until its frame, which satisfies this). — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:168, :198-203
FACT: StartSession takes no pod-level lock around Runtime.Start or the confirmation today; the only guard the ack wait holds is the per-slot guard openRuntimeSession acquires, so a waiting start does not serialize co-tenant starts. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:86 (StartSession body: writeSessionManifest, Runtime.Start, noteRuntimeStarted, no Lock/ops calls)

### [non-spec.4.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: In every real flow a pending acknowledgement gate implies the session's per-slot guard is held by the open sequence (CODE-1 item 1 step a, or Resume's guard held from ahead of its claim), so "slot guard acquirable while the ack is pending" is unreachable without poking gate state directly. A sender caller can wait on the gate with the guard free only while the gate is `not started`, e.g. with StartSession parked in Runtime.Start after its claim — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:20 (step a), spec-changes.md:321 (Edit 12), pkg/adapter/resume.go:58-62
FACT: acquireSlotGuardForResolve refuses only an identifier under a reclaim hold; otherwise it blocks on the guard under ctx, so a mid-session FinalizeWorkspace issued during a pending open sequence waits on the guard, not on the gate — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/bindattempt.go:256-267, pkg/adapter/staging.go:219-224
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess.broadcast sends to each subscriber sequentially and send blocks on a full 64-line feed until the subscriber is done, so an unread, uncancelled Output subscription stalls the shared reader for every session; the open-sequence subscription is safe only because waitCtx is cancelled at return — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:166-203, :405-416, :491-494
FACT: Output on SocketRuntimeProcess ignores the session argument and fails when not connected; Runtime.Start guarantees connected before the open sequence — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:267-297, :475-496
DECISION: examined the CODE-9 gate (wakeups, Read only from pending, released-terminal, Failed after step d, connection drop mid-wait, bounded sender waits, co-tenant cost) and found it coherent apart from the TEST-1 row 3 guard assertion — BECAUSE every waiter is woken by reset/read/failed/released or its deadline, and no wait sits under s.ops or a guard — ALTERNATIVES: reporting the unconditional subscribe-before-step-c as a stall hazard; rejected because a defer-cancelled waitCtx (go vet lostcancel) removes it at return.

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]

FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess fan-out is not head-of-line-free despite its comments: `subscriber.send` blocks the shared `fanOut` reader once a subscriber's 64-line `feed` is full and its consumer is not draining `out` (unbuffered), until the subscriber is cancelled. A CODE-9 item 2 wait subscription opened before step c is undrained only for the duration of the `session_start` write and is cancelled at return (govet lostcancel forces the `waitCtx` cancel), so the stall window is bounded and was not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:166-205, :405-415
FACT: Every caller of the six session-scoped senders is one of Checkpoint (RequestCheckpoint, CompleteCheckpoint), Interrupt (interruptViaLifecycle), FinalizeWorkspace (SignalFilesUpdated), rotateProviderFull, and SignalDeadline; CODE-9 item 3 lists all of them. EVIDENCE: grep of pkg/ cmd/ tests/testinfra non-test files; pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:163-167, lifecycle.go:95,114, staging.go:315, credentials.go:207
FACT: The gateway bounds the SignalDeadline RPC by its interrupt deadline (`context.WithTimeout(ctx, s.interruptDeadline())`), so a gate wait bounded by `remainingMs` (about 300 s) cannot stall the watchdog's sweep. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/expiry_warning.go:62-66
FACT: InProcessRuntime binds one session at a time (Start errors for a second session), so the gate's co-tenant cost exists only on the socket transport. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/embedded.go:63-71
FACT: A StartSession failure on the base-mode bind path reclaims the pod (`reclaimOnFailure`), and on the slot path it records a slot failure. An acknowledgement-timeout start failure therefore uses the shipped failure path, and no store write is new. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindlaunch.go:52-77, slotbinder.go:373-382
DECISION: No findings under the performance and reliability lens — BECAUSE the proposal adds no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write; StartSession takes no pod-wide lock; every gate waiter has a deadline; senders wait outside s.ops and the per-slot guard; and Postgres failover, Redis reset, and coordinator handoff reach only the request ctx, whose cancellation ends the wait on the existing error arm — ALTERNATIVES: filing the pre-Begin checkpoint gate error returning before `barrier.link` (it leaves a drain barrier unlinked, unlike today's post-link RequestCheckpoint failure), rejected because it is reachable only for a drain barrier on a session whose start acknowledgement is still pending, and the barrier ends as it does for a stream that never opened.
USEFUL [Gate entry sources], [Waiter bounds], [No pod-wide lock in `StartSession`]: these entries let the lock and bound audit confirm the design rather than re-derive it.

### [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]
FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess fan-out is NOT isolated per subscriber despite its comment: broadcast calls subscriber.send sequentially and send blocks on a full 64-slot feed until done, and pump blocks on an unread out. One subscriber that nobody reads stalls the single reader, so every co-tenant Attach stream (and its heartbeat_ack) stalls. CODE-9 item 6 copies these semantics to InProcessRuntime and SubprocessExecutor. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:197-202 (send), :177-190 (pump), :405-416 (broadcast)
WATCHOUT: CODE-9 item 2 subscribes before step c unconditionally and defines waitCtx's end only by the request deadline and SessionStartAckTimeout; a non-waiting start "reads nothing" and nothing cancels the subscription at return. Filed as a finding (subscribe only when waiting, cancel on every return). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:212
FACT: heartbeat ack window is 10 s (defaultHeartbeatAckTimeout), so a stall of up to SessionStartAckTimeout (<10 s) on top of a pending heartbeat can escalate a co-tenant as hung. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go:17
FACT: gate mechanism otherwise checked under reliability: every slot removal goes through deregisterSlotLocked (slotsession.go:204), every failed start arm is followed by an entry removal, waiter bounds all derive from deadlines, and Interrupt/Checkpoint pre-Begin placement matches the tree (lifecycle.go:44, checkpoint.go:111). No other finding.

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding at non-spec round 4 — BECAUSE every control the lens covers is intact: CODE-7 refuses an empty nonce and installs only a passing connection (acceptLoop today accepts once, pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:331-350, so the loop-on-failure adds no replacement path); DemoteSDK fails closed per owner; the gate's error arms map to existing fail-closed branches; no CRD, RBAC, NetworkPolicy, or store write — ALTERNATIVES: filing the overridable --session-start-ack-timeout above the 10 s hold-timeout window was rejected, because an act without the serialization counts as failed (SPEC-3 Edit 12) and failed cleanup acts are held for the pod's life, which is fail-closed.
FACT: the rotation path's 60 s ack window bounds the gate wait too (ackCtx, pkg/adapter/credentials.go:204-207), so a pending gate does not lengthen the exposure of a revoked credential beyond today's bound. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/credentials.go:197-221
USEFUL [Direct-mode self-report]: CODE-3 item 5's runtime-chosen sessionId on llm_request_completed is the only bound sourced from a pod self-report; it is the accepted §11.2 direct-mode residual and should not be refiled.

### [non-spec.4.review-single-source.1]
DECISION: no single-source finding in non-spec round 4 — BECAUSE every gate rule (CODE-9 item 1 lifecycle, item 2 wait and failure handling, item 3 branches, caller lock lines, and bounds) has one stating site; TEST-1 rows 2 and 3 cite those homes structurally, and the summary's decisions 9, 11, 28, and 30 each carry their reasoning at that one site — ALTERNATIVES: filing the per-frame no-answer outcome list (spec-changes Edge cases bullet beside CODE-9 item 3's "On an error" list) was rejected, because both describe existing tree behavior for two different triggers (the post-session_end residual and a gate error), and the spec home (SPEC-4 Edit 2) states only "ends as it ends for a frame the runtime does not answer".
WATCHOUT: CODE-4 item 9 says "The adapter writes a session's events only after it reads the session's `session_started`" with no rule-3 not-awaiting exception. It is a one-clause reason for "no early-event queue", and the SDK's drop behavior is correct either way, so it was not filed. If a later round rewrites that item, add the exception or cut the clause to a SPEC-4 Edit 2 citation. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 9; spec-changes.md SPEC-4 Edit 2 "except for a session whose start did not wait".
FACT: the tree's `SignalDeadline` takes `_ context.Context` (pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go:79-ish, "func (s *Server) SignalDeadline(_ context.Context"), so CODE-9 item 3's "passes its request context bounded by remainingMs" requires naming the parameter; this is plumbing and not a defect. Interrupt's `s.ops.Begin(ctx, opInterrupt, "")` is pod-wide, and Checkpoint's is keyed by sessionID (pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go:44, pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:111), which matches Settled **`s.ops` users**.
USEFUL [non-spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]: the by-entry `awaitSessionStarted` binding and the "no waiting call under s.ops or a guard" constraint held up under a concurrency read. The waiter-wakeup constraint in item 1 covers the not-started→pending→not-awaiting path and the released-between-b-and-reset path, and the open-sequence Read runs only from pending, so a released gate stays released when a late ack is read.

Between subscribe and step c's write, the stall window is bounded by one WriteEnvelope. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:166-203, :405-414
FACT: The lock-placement subtest's not-started state is reachable for every sender. StartSession holds no slot guard while parked in Runtime.Start (the guard is taken in open-sequence step a), the mid-session FinalizeWorkspace resolve admits an unstarted entry (allowStarted only relaxes the started rule), and Checkpoint resolves bound entries before s.ops.Begin. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:117-170, pkg/adapter/slot.go:146, pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:94-111
FACT: rotateProviderFull maps only context.DeadlineExceeded with a live parent ctx to the fallback. Any other error goes to EmitLeaseRejected, so the new failed-gate RotateCredentials subtest discriminates. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/credentials.go:204-229

### [non-spec.7.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: close "no test pins the gate waking every concurrent waiter" with one coverage clause in TEST-1 row 3 (`TestSessionStartedGatesRuntimeOpsAndBoundsTheStart_spec_28_5_3`, tier 1): two CODE-9 item 3 callers waiting at once on one pending gate, once per transition that ends pending (Read, Failed, Released), asserting each returns as CODE-9 item 1's Owner constraint states before its own ctx ends. The clause points at the constraint and does not restate it. BECAUSE every existing case has one waiter, so a wake-one primitive (sync.Cond.Signal, one buffered send) passes them all. ALTERNATIVES: extend the 7a row 2 (`TestSessionFramesStayPairedAcrossReclaimAndRetry`) with a second concurrent sender — rejected, row 2 already carries the pairing and ordering invariants and a wakeup miss there shows only as a returned-within-bound SignalDeadline, which row 2 already admits; replace IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE with a named primitive (close a channel per transition) — rejected, the caller directive keeps plumbing as implementor's choice and the constraint already states the requirement.
WATCHOUT: the discriminating assertion is that BOTH waiters return before their own ctx deadline; each waiter's ctx must outlast the transition (for Failed, outlast `SessionStartAckTimeout`), or a missed wakeup looks identical to a correct error return. A tier-1 test cannot observe that both goroutines are parked before the transition; a waiter that arrives late re-reads and passes, so the test can only false-pass, never false-fail. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-9 item 1 Owner, item 3 waiting branch and closing paragraph.
WATCHOUT: a pending gate exists only inside the open sequence, which holds the per-slot guard; callers waiting on it hold no guard (CODE-9 item 3), so two waiters on a pending gate are reachable at tier 1. Use callers that take no `s.ops` before the wait (SignalDeadline, RotateCredentials, or Checkpoint before `Begin`).

### [non-spec.7.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: `SocketRuntimeProcess` fan-out gives each subscriber a 64-frame buffered feed, and `broadcast` blocks on a full feed (`send` selects only feed or done), so an unread live subscription stalls delivery to every co-tenant after 64 frames. The socket.go comment "A full buffer blocks only this subscriber's pump" is inaccurate. This is why CODE-9 item 2 cancels the wait subscription on every return. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:166-203, :405-415
FACT: `claimSessionSlot` sets `st.started = true` at the claim, before `Runtime.Start`, and refuses a second fresh claim on a started entry (only the idempotent repeat passes). So exactly one open sequence runs per entry, and the gate cannot go from read or not awaiting back to pending. Checkpoint's second, non-waiting `awaitSessionStarted` call after `s.ops.Begin` is therefore sound. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:110-123
FACT: `deregisterSlotLocked` is the only `delete(s.slots, …)` in non-test code, so CODE-9 item 1's Released bullet ("the one step every removal of an entry takes") holds. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:204-212
FACT: `s.ops` is taken only by Checkpoint (per session) and Interrupt (pod-wide). FinalizeWorkspace takes the per-slot guard through `acquireSlotGuardForResolve` with `defer releaseGuard()`, so CODE-9 item 3's "after it releases the slot guard" needs a restructure. The "co-tenant's upload" in TEST-1 row 3 means a co-tenant checkpoint's chunk upload, not staging. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:111, pkg/adapter/lifecycle.go:44, pkg/adapter/staging.go:219-223
DECISION: no findings this round. BECAUSE the gate lifecycle, the per-caller lock constraints, the bounded waits, the connection-drop path (subscriber `out` closes on EOF, which counts as "the wait ends without the frame"), and the Attach filter (a separate fan-out subscriber) all trace through without a defect. ALTERNATIVES: I weighed filing that the subscription may be opened before step a, which would stall co-tenants during the guard wait, and that the new TEST-1 subscription clause asserts an Output context on step a/b returns that may make no Output call. Both are implementor-latitude ambiguities below the bar.
USEFUL [Settled **`s.ops` users.**, Trap **Guard holders and the gate.**]: these saved re-deriving the lock map.

### [non-spec.9.review-performance.1]
FACT: `SubprocessExecutor.session` releases `e.mu` after publishing the child in `procs`, and `Send` takes `sess.mu` only afterwards, so two concurrent first `Send`s for one session can reach `sess.mu` in either order; the gateway's developer-loop delivery (`deliverMessageBatch`) takes no per-session lock before `executor.Send`. CODE-2 item 2's "only when `session` spawned the child in that call" therefore lets the non-spawning `Send` write its `message` before the spawning one writes `session_start`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/subprocess.go:138-145, :309-336; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:146, :210
DECISION: filed the CODE-2 ordering race (fix: write the frame inside `session` before the child is added to `procs`, on the `Send` path only) — BECAUSE after CODE-4 item 3 the SDK answers a message for a session it does not hold with `RUNTIME_ERROR`, which is the failure summary decision 17 says CODE-2 exists to prevent — ALTERNATIVES: a per-session lock in the gateway (larger, outside CODE-2's target).
FACT: The Go SDK's `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` lifecycle loop runs each callback inline (`handleCheckpoint` calls `onCheckpoint` before reading the next frame), so after CODE-4 one session's slow `OnCheckpoint` or `OnCredentialsRotated` delays co-tenants' `credentials_rotated` and `deadline_approaching`. Not filed: adapter-side `opLock` already serializes checkpoint and interrupt pod-wide (`running bool`), and the remaining delay is bounded by the rotation 60 s fallback. — EVIDENCE: sdks/runtime/go/runtime/lifecycle.go:152-187, pkg/adapter/oplock.go:62-95
UNVERIFIED: CODE-4 item 4 removes a session from "its routing table" on `session_end`, while item 2 places the state in both the routing table and "the lifecycle router"; no item says the lifecycle-router entry is removed. Item 9's "does not hold" implies it; a client-surface or mechanism lens may decide whether that is explicit enough.

DECISION: CODE-2 item 1's concurrent-Send ordering moves out of TEST-1 row 4 (tier 1, now sequential Sends only) into one new tier-7a TEST-1 row placed directly after row 4: many fresh sessions on one exported `executor.NewSubprocessExecutor`, several concurrent first `Send`s each, a capture child that answers only `message` lines; per session the first captured line is the only `session_start` — BECAUSE test-coverage.md maps ordering under concurrency to 7a, and a one-shot run cannot tell a correct implementation from the excluded one — ALTERNATIVES: "many iterations" inside the tier-1 row (puts process-spawning stress in the unit tier, against the tier mapping); extending the 7a row `TestSessionFramesStayPairedAcrossReclaimAndRetry` (adapter component, different subject); a deterministic internal hook (the ordering mechanism is IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE, so a hook would fix the mechanism).

WATCHOUT: the detection is probabilistic. The window in which a second `Send` can beat the spawning one to `sess.mu` is narrow, so the row uses several concurrent Sends per session over many sessions. ### [non-spec.11.review-test-coverage.1]
DECISION: filed one finding. CODE-2 item 1's new concurrent-`Send` ordering constraint is pinned only by a tier-1 clause in TEST-1 row 4, which runs the concurrent pair once. Under the tree's locking, the first `Send` almost always wins `sess.mu`, so a spawned-in-this-call flag implementation passes. Concurrency and ordering map to tier 7a under test-coverage.md, and no 7a case exists for it. BECAUSE the clause was written in round 10 and does not discriminate. ALTERNATIVES: refiling the CODE-6 item 1 harness `timeout` category (rejected four times, see [non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1]); Python/TS concurrency at 7a (tier 7a is Go-only, and per-SDK ordering is mirrored by the "Go cases above, per SDK" row); CODE-9 attach.go and CODE-4 item 9 (both on the refuted list).
FACT: The race window CODE-2 item 1 names lies between `session()` releasing `e.mu` (deferred unlock) and `Send` taking `sess.mu`. A concurrent second `Send` blocks on `e.mu` for the whole spawn, so it almost never reaches `sess.mu` first in one run. A single-run test of a violating implementation therefore passes. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/subprocess.go:138-143, :309-336
FACT: Tier 1 already runs with `-race` (cmd/lenny-test/cmd_run.go:880), but the race detector does not detect a lock-ordered logical reordering. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/cmd_run.go:880
FACT: The only executor tests are subprocess_test.go (cmd/runtimes/echo, which ignores session_start), subprocess_crash_test.go, and the tier-3 envelope test, so CODE-2 de-pins no other line-indexed capture. EVIDENCE: grep NewSubprocessExecutor

DECISION: returned no findings under the end-to-end mechanism lens in non-spec round 16 — BECAUSE the r14-prefix diff only deletes the summary Watch-out bullet on CODE-3's interim harness order (no dangling reference remains; CODE-3 item 4 and CODE-6 item 1 still own it), and tracing CODE-2, CODE-5, CODE-6, and CODE-8 as deliverables found no broken flow — ALTERNATIVES: CODE-6 item 1's harness-wait constraint references CODE-9 item 2's default, which is chosen two steps later (S16 before S18); not filed because CODE-9 item 2's own constraint caps the default below 10 s, so a harness wait of at least 10 s satisfies both whatever CODE-9 picks; CODE-9 Targets `attach.go` still listed (non-spec-changes.md:198, :323) but refuted four times as immaterial, not refiled; CODE-4 item 9 no longer states the ordering unconditionally (non-spec-changes.md:82), so the caller's carry-over no longer holds.
FACT: `SubprocessExecutor.readResponse` skips every non-`response` line, so a `session_started` from a `Send`-spawned SDK child never reaches `Send`; `Send` and `Output` are never used on the same child (gateway dev loop uses `Send`, the adapter `--runtime-bin` path uses `WriteEnvelope`+`Output`), so CODE-9 item 6's per-process fan-out reader does not collide with `readResponse` if started lazily from `Output`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/subprocess.go:234-305; cmd/lenny-gateway/runtime_select.go:42; cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:416

FACT: `SocketRuntimeProcess.Output` ignores its sessionID and broadcasts every line to every subscriber; the wait's match on the per-Server unique `startId` alone is therefore sufficient. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:475-496

### [non-spec.16.review-performance.1]
FACT: The only change since r14 is the deletion of the summary Watch-out bullet restating CODE-3 item 4's interim harness order; nothing in non-spec-changes.md moved. EVIDENCE: diff -ru of non-spec-r14-prefix against the proposal dir.
FACT: SubprocessExecutor.Send reads the child's stdout through readResponse's own goroutine over the same bufio.Scanner that Output scans, and no gateway code calls SubprocessExecutor.Output, so CODE-9 item 6's fan-out reader stays correct for the developer loop only if it starts lazily at the first Output call. SocketRuntimeProcess starts its reader eagerly on connection. Not filed, because existing subprocess_test.go Send cases fail on an eager reader. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/subprocess.go:138-171, :234-256, :259-305; pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:274, :320.
FACT: The manifest (and its mcpNonce) is first written by writeSessionManifest inside StartSession, Resume, and the SDK-warm ConfigureWorkspace; CODE-8 keeps that per-start rewrite, so the nonce still rotates per start. Only a CH-RUNTIMEOPS redial reads the rotated value, because the CH-MSGSOCK socket accepts one connection for the pod's life. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:138, pkg/adapter/resume.go:138, pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:238, pkg/adapter/manifest.go:280-320.
DECISION: no performance finding. BECAUSE no etcd, Postgres, or Redis write is added, the ack wait is adapter-local, sits under the per-slot guard only, and has a bound below 10 s; the co-tenant fan-out cost is recorded in Settled **Socket fan-out stall** and **`Output` scope**; and CODE-2, CODE-5, CODE-6, and CODE-8 add no shared lock or per-unit write. ALTERNATIVES: I did not re-file the CODE-9 attach.go target entry, which the material skeptic has refuted four times. CODE-4 item 9 no longer states the ordering, so that finding no longer holds.

A client therefore cannot forge a `session_start` with a co-tenant's `credentialsPath`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:89, pkg/adapter/attach.go:262

The window is pre-existing and only less strict, and the ack wait runs only while that session's start is pending.

### [non-spec.18.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens — BECAUSE the r16-to-r18 diff only adds a full_test.go fixture sentence to CODE-6 item 1 and trims TEST-1 row 3's constraint list; no store write is added (Standing **No store writes** holds), every wait stays bounded, and CODE-2, CODE-5, CODE-6, and CODE-8 add only intra-pod work — ALTERNATIVES: filing CODE-9 item 6's drop-when-unsubscribed fan-out for embedded and subprocess as a frame-loss regression; rejected because the gateway's Attach binds with an empty first frame (pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go:872-876) and Attach subscribes to Output before its recv loop writes any envelope (pkg/adapter/attach.go:59-98), so no response is written before a subscriber exists on the normal path.
WATCHOUT: every Full check in cmd/lenny-compliance runs its runtime under a hard-coded 10 s process context (full.go:286, :325, :365, :403, :450), and the checks' reads are each bounded at 3 s. CODE-6 item 1's harness wait must be no shorter than CODE-9's adapter default, which is capped only below 10 s. A default near 10 s therefore leaves the check little or no budget, and the stub could be killed (EOF) before the harness wait ends. Not filed, because a default of a few seconds fits, but whoever picks the CODE-9 default should keep it well under 10 s or widen the per-check context. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-compliance/full.go:286-304
FACT: SDK-warm exists only on the embedded transport (SDKWarmInProcessRuntime; ConfigureWorkspace calls Start). The CH-MSGSOCK no-manifest refusal therefore cannot deadlock an SDK-warm pre-connect over the socket. The socket accept loop also starts only at the first Runtime.Start, so on a pod-warm sidecar the manifest wait moves no work that was earlier done at warm time. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/embedded_sdkwarm.go:50-51, pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:303
USEFUL [Socket fan-out stall], [Output scope]: these confirmed that the wait's subscription costs co-tenants only while unread, so I did not need to re-trace them.

- FACT: The gateway issues ConfigureWorkspace and StartSession once per Launch with no client-side retry; any error reclaims the pod. A concurrent idempotent ConfigureWorkspace repeat (fresh=false) arriving while the fresh call's open sequence is still in the ack wait therefore does not arise in the tree, so the repeat arm "calls nothing and writes nothing" is not a retry hazard. - FACT: SocketRuntimeProcess starts its fan-out reader at Start (not first Output) and drops lines while no subscriber is registered, so CODE-9 item 6's fan-out for InProcessRuntime and SubprocessExecutor dropping lines between the wait's cancel and Attach's subscribe matches the socket transport's existing semantics. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go:274, :320, :364-376, :392-416
- FACT: On the acknowledgement-failure path no rule-8 record is taken, so the caller's releaseClaimedSlot teardown sees r.live false and tearDownReclaimedSlot writes no second session_end. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 3 and CODE-9 item 2
- DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE the CODE-2, CODE-5, CODE-6, CODE-8, and CODE-9 recovery paths traced (child exit and Close in SubprocessExecutor, OnCreate hang versus session_end rule 2, ack timeout then same-pod retry, hold-timeout pass 1, DemoteSDK guard expiry) each end bounded or are recorded as accepted residuals in Standing context — ALTERNATIVES: refiling attach.go in CODE-9 Targets and the CODE-4 item 9 exception was rejected because both are in the refuted list and CODE-4 item 9 no longer restates the ordering.
- WATCHOUT: CODE-4 item 4 cancels the session context from the session goroutine, so an OnCreate still running when session_end arrives is not cancelled; this conforms to staged session_end rule 2 ("once its creation ends"), so do not file it as a reliability defect. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:251

### [redesign.9.harness-read.1]
DECISION (introspection redesign, 2026-10-04): CODE-6 item 1 is rewritten as the one whole specification of the compliance harness's `session_started` read. It names a shared helper in `full.go` (`readSessionStarted`) that every Full check writing a session-scoped `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frame calls after `handshake` and the CODE-3 item 4 prefix, and that `checkRuntimeOpsHandshake` uses for item 8's read. It fixes the bound as `sessionStartedWait = 10 * time.Second` and widens each Full check's process context to that wait plus the former 10 s. On expiry the check returns an error wrapping `errSessionStartedWaitExpired`, and `recordCheck` records `Pass=false` with no failure category. The item 8 stub test asserts that sentinel on each check that sends a session-scoped frame, with the wait passed in short. CODE-3 item 4 stays the home of the interim order and CODE-9 item 2 the home of the adapter's default bound; CODE-6 item 1 points to both. BECAUSE the harness precondition drew a confirmed finding in three consecutive sweeps, one facet at a time: the summary copy of the interim order (round 14, [non-spec.14.fix-G1.1]), the untested precondition (round 16, [non-spec.16.fix-G1.1]), and the unimplementable `timeout` category assertion (round 18, [non-spec.18.fix-G1.1]), while the wait budget recurred as a WATCHOUT or an OPEN in rounds 1, 16, and 18 ([non-spec.1.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.16.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.16.review-test-coverage.1], [non-spec.18.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.18.review-performance.1]). An IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE wait with a forward constraint left the helper, the budget, the expiry outcome, and the test each for a later sweep to find. ALTERNATIVES: a bound stated relative to the check's deadline (rejected: it shrinks by whatever `waitConn` and the handshake consume, so inside today's 10 s context it cannot guarantee a wait as long as an adapter default that may approach 10 s); keeping the 10 s context with a wait of a few seconds (rejected: it needs S18's value at S16 and fails a runtime the adapter accepts if S18 picks a default above it); a `--session-started-wait` flag (rejected: the value follows from CODE-9 item 2's cap, and a shorter setting rejects runtimes the adapter accepts); a package-level wait variable the test overrides (rejected: global mutable state; the checks take the wait as a value).
FACT: every Full check runs its runtime under `context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)`, `waitConn` and each `recvJSONLine` take up to 3 s, the checks ignore the battery's `--timeout` value (default 30 s), and `checkDeadlineSignal` reads stdout through the scanner `spawn` returns. A 10 s acknowledgement wait inside that context ends at EOF when the child is killed. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-compliance/full.go `checkRuntimeOpsHandshake`, `checkCheckpointQuiesce`, `checkInterruptAck`, `checkCredentialRotation`, `checkDeadlineSignal`, `waitConn`, `recvJSONLine`, `spawn`; cmd/lenny-compliance/main.go `timeout` flag
CORRECTS [non-spec.16.review-test-coverage.1]: its OPEN on whether CODE-6 item 1's harness-wait constraint is satisfiable at S16 is closed; the 10 s `sessionStartedWait` is no shorter than any default CODE-9 item 2 allows.
CORRECTS [Standing context Traps "Harness wait constraint"]: "met by any wait of 10 s or more" ignored the fixed 10 s per-check process context; the Trap now records the widened context.
WATCHOUT: do not move the bound into CODE-9, restate CODE-3 item 4's interim order in CODE-6, list the check names in item 1's test sentence, or drop "answer every session-scoped `CH-RUNTIMEOPS` frame" from it ([non-spec.16.fix-G1.1]). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 1
